# OSCRSJ Session History

> Lossless archive of every Cowork session that touched this repo. CLAUDE.md (the living file) carries the 3 most recent sessions full + every session from the last 14 days as pointers + an Open Follow-ups punch list. Historical detail lives here, grep-accessed.
>
> **Anchor convention:** every session entry has a stable block-ref ID `^session-NN-short-slug` resolvable from CLAUDE.md and from cross-references in commit messages or other docs. NN is zero-padded to 2 digits (Session 03, Session 27, Session 46) for grep stability.
>
> **Two access patterns:** (1) sequential — grep `^### Session NN` for any session by number, or scan the chronological Index below; (2) topical — scan the Topic Index for all sessions touching a given workstream (reviewer pipeline, email infrastructure, editorial decisions, etc.).
>
> **Find a session:** `grep -nE "^### Session [0-9]+" docs/session-history.md` for the chronological index, or `grep -nE "^### " docs/session-history.md | head -30` to scan section heads + topics.

## Topic Index

> Curated by topic, not chronology. Every session is listed under each workstream it touched (a single session may appear in 2-3 topics — that's correct). Newest anchors first within each topic. Update at every wrap-up: rule M1 step 2 in [[CLAUDE.md Refactor#§9 Add/move rules]].

## Cross-cutting topics (superseded specs / version history)

> Documentation/spec content that has been replaced by newer values. Each topic carries its full historical timeline as a single block, NOT one entry per session. Each block is anchored stably (e.g., `^design-system-history`) so CLAUDE.md's current-state reference tables can link out for "how did we get here."

### Design system version history  ^design-system-history
- **v2.1 (Clean Journal, 2026-04-18, commit `0ba6db4`)** — first white-body shift. `cream` was `#FFF5EB`, `border` strengthened to 0.18, 3-tier hierarchy (dark → cream → white).
- **v2.2 (Journal Ink, 2026-04-18 later same day)** — introduced `ink` token (`#1a1410`) as primary body text color. Set as HTML body default. Replaced earlier reliance on `text-tan` for body text (banned for normal text from this point — 3.53:1 contrast on cream fails WCAG AA).
- **v2.3 (Reading Mode, 2026-04-21 Session 16, commits `fb593ae` + revert)** — `cream` → `#FDFBF8` (barely-warm near-white, ~99% luminance), `ink` → `#120D08` (warmer near-black, retains brown hint), `cream-alt` → `#F8F4ED`. Source Serif 4 body-serif pairing shipped + reverted same session per Kanwar — body stays on Inter 16px/1.65.
- **Background color rule (ratified 2026-04-23)** — full-bleed section wrappers → `bg-white` (no tint-band strips); subtle highlights (chips, pills, step-circles, table headers, empty states, hover) → `bg-cream-alt`. `bg-cream` is now near-invisible; reach for it only when surface should read "almost the body background".
- *[current values live in CLAUDE.md §7 Design System]*

### APC pricing model history  ^apc-pricing-history
- **Original 4-phase roadmap** (locked 2026-04-11) — `$0 → $299 → $499 → $699` over Months 1-6 / 7-18 / Year 2-3 / Post-PubMed.
- **Original 6-tier waiver policy** — 100% LMIC / 50% lower-middle-income / 50% PGY-1/2 + med students / 25% first-ever-publication / financial hardship / first-time-author.
- **Replaced 2026-04-26 (Session 26 Franklin, commit `fbb7cf6`)** — single $499 standard rate after free-publish window (manuscripts submitted before August 1, 2026 publish free); 6-tier waiver table dropped; replaced by case-by-case discount inquiry form on `/apc`.
- **Routing flip 2026-04-26 (Session 27, commit `b0a279b`)** — discount inquiry + contact forms wired to server actions writing to `audit_logs` + dual Resend emails (internal notification to dept-aliased inbox + confirmation to inquirer with editorial Reply-To).
- **Mailbox-void quick-fix 2026-05-05 (Session 46, commit `7d575ed`)** — `oscrsj.com` apex has NO MX records; every send to `info@`/`submit@`/`editorial@`/`waivers@oscrsj.com` bounces. `DISCOUNT_INQUIRY_INBOX` and `CONTACT_INBOXES` map all flipped to `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` until Google Workspace is provisioned.
- *[current values live in CLAUDE.md §8 Business Context]*

### Editorial-decision enum split history  ^decision-enum-history
- **Pre-Session-13** — single `reject` enum value on `editorial_decision_type`; single `desk_rejected` value on `manuscript_status` (no distinction between desk-reject and post-review-reject).
- **Session 13 (2026-04-21, migration 011)** — split `editorial_decision_type` to add `post_review_reject` (legacy `reject` rows remain valid, advisory NOTICE block surfaces them); added `rejected` to `manuscript_status` distinct from `desk_rejected`.
- *[current state: see migration `011_session_13_columns.sql`]*

### Reviewer form structure history  ^reviewer-form-history
- **Original (Session 10, 2026-04-18)** — 6 Likert scales (Quality / Novelty / Rigor / Data / Clarity / Scope fit) + Recommendation + 2-textarea structure (Comments-to-author + Confidential-comments-to-editor).
- **Session 32 (commit `ea85525`, 2026-04-26)** — collapsed two textareas to single "Feedback and review" box. Comments-to-author UI section + 200-word counter + 200-word minimum all removed; server-side gate dropped.
- **Session 33 (commit `1a56a85`, 2026-04-26)** — flipped writes from `comments_to_editor` → `comments_to_author` (Session 32's design left `comments_to_author` empty by design which broke `loadRevisionContext` → `RevisionStep0` author-facing display). Editor read-only review view collapsed two separate sections into single "Feedback and review" card.
- **Session 35 (commit `baaa7e7`, 2026-04-26)** — removed all 6 Likert scales (`LIKERT_SCALES` array, render block, state fields, validation gates, server-side checks, editor-notification email score block, anonymised review payload, author Step 0 score grid).
- **Session 46 (commit `2452054`, 2026-05-05)** — Reviewer Word-template retired; `/for-reviewers` rebranded "Guide for Reviewers"; review-structure shifted to overall-comment (1-2 paragraphs, no line numbers) + section-by-section line-numbered review.
- *[current state: `app/review/[token]/form/ReviewSubmissionForm.tsx` + `/for-reviewers` page]*

### Editorial inbox routing history  ^inbox-routing-history
- **Original** — every transactional notification routed to `kanwarparhar@gmail.com` (hardcoded fallback in `lib/email/resend.ts`).
- **Session 43 (2026-05-04)** — `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` env-var pattern introduced (Reply-To routes editorial replies back to inquirer). Initial Vercel env state was incorrectly recorded as `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` — verified via Chrome MCP in Session 46 to be unset.
- **Session 46 partial flip (commit `d1265fe`, 2026-05-05)** — 5 hardcoded constants flipped (`EDITORIAL_NOTIFY_EMAIL`, `ADMIN_NOTIFY_EMAIL`, `INTERNAL_EDITORIAL_EMAIL`, `DEFAULT_REPLY_TO` fallback, `digestRecipient()` third rung) → `oscrsjournal@gmail.com`. End-to-end smoke test verified.
- **Session 46 mailbox-void quick-fix (commit `7d575ed`)** — DNS `dig MX oscrsj.com` returned NXDOMAIN; contact + APC forms also flipped to `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` until Workspace provisioning.
- *[current state: `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` is the canonical editorial inbox; flip back to per-department aliases when Google Workspace lands]*

### Audience-reframe history  ^audience-reframe-history
- **Pre-Session-25** — site copy spoke to "medical students, residents, and fellows" (trainee-only framing).
- **Session 25 (2026-04-26 Franklin sitewide consistency sweep)** — locked "the global orthopedic surgery community" sitewide across `/about`, `/aims-scope`, `/faq`, `/submit`, `/for-reviewers`, `/for-reviewers/apply`, `/indexing`, homepage CTA, `/media` boilerplate, `app/layout.tsx` metadata (4 surfaces — Google snippets + OG + Twitter + JSON-LD Organization). Locked phrasing: "practicing surgeons, fellows, residents, students, and researchers."
- *[current values live in CLAUDE.md §8 Business Context]*

### Turnaround-time language history  ^turnaround-time-history
- **Original** — varied across pages, some surfaces claimed 7-day decision / 30-day publication.
- **Session 25 morning** — Commit D pushed "10 / 35" turnaround promise. Re-verted same day.
- **Session 25 afternoon (commit `0bdc5f5`)** — reverted to original `1-7 / 1-3 / 14-21 / 1-3` `/peer-review` stage breakdown after Kanwar clarified "10 days" was always the EIC SCREENING response, not full peer-reviewed decision. Homepage stat rewritten as "10 days from submission to initial editorial response". Per-type review timelines reverted on `/guide-for-authors` (Case Report 2-3 wk, Case Series 3-4 wk, Surgical Technique 2-3 wk, Images 7-10 d, Letter 1-2 wk, Review 4-6 wk).
- *[current state: see `/peer-review` + `/guide-for-authors` + homepage]*

### Session 78 — 2026-06-10 — Sushant Cowork — Cleanup-and-hardening sweep: SANRA live-404 fix + verapdf PASS confirmed + launchd auto-start fixed (3 root causes) + bearer auth on /api/publish/[id] + C2 JATS consent dedupe + Narrative Review backend + PRISMA hard-gate — 3 OSCRSJ commits `a7abef0` + `f925127` + `9e9afd2`, 4 renderer commits `569a1ba` + `295ade2` + `e304e3a` + `3accc3b`  ^session-78-cleanup-hardening

Kanwar brief: resolve every open submission-portal and PDF-publishing issue in one hardening pass, phase by phase, commit at each boundary. Run on the Mac directly (no Cowork sandbox). Phases 0–7 complete; Phase 8 interactive smokes + migration 025 Studio run pending at wrap time.

**Phase 0 — preflight.** Tree count 306 (legitimate growth vs the ~196-200 Git Safety baseline — composition app/public/lib/supabase, no junk; the danger sign is a *shrunken* tree). Baseline `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0. Three manuscript working files (`Final Manuscript V2 - Revised.docx` + 2 Tarong title pages) moved out of the repo root to `~/Documents/OSCRSJ/manuscripts/` per Kanwar choice. Stale 0-byte `.git/index.lock` (09:45 same-day) swept to `.git/.stale-junk/`.

**Phase 1 — SANRA PDF live-404 fix (`a7abef0`).** `public/downloads/oscrsj-sanra-self-rating.pdf` existed on disk since ~Session 66 era but was never committed → production 404 on two linking surfaces. PDF content-verified before commit (OSCRSJ branding, six SANRA items with 0/1/2 anchors, Baethge 2019 CC BY attribution, page-2 fillable score sheet — no content changes needed, Janine not flagged). Committed PDF + `scripts/build-sanra-self-rating.py`; post-deploy curl 200; `/templates` + `/guide-for-authors` each reference the path twice.

**Phase 2 — verapdf real determination: PASS.** Downloaded the e0001 render report from Storage — actual path is `published_pdf/{manuscriptId}_render-report.json` (the §11 item's `published/{id}/render-report.json` path was stale). `verapdf.result === "pass"`, conformance PDF/A-1B, failures `[]`, warnings `[]`. First real verapdf determination since the Session 69 C3 parser fix — the published PDF genuinely conforms.

**Phase 3 — launchd renderer auto-start fixed (`569a1ba`).** THREE stacked root causes, not one: (1) launchd refused to exec `scripts/launchd-start.sh` directly — the file carries a SIP-protected `com.apple.provenance` xattr from its Cowork-sandbox origin ("Operation not permitted"); (2) the plist's `WorkingDirectory` key pointing into `~/Documents` triggered the `getcwd` TCC errors before the script even ran; (3) once running, the plist's `NODE_ENV=production` made the wrapper's `npm install` omit-AND-PRUNE devDependencies — every launchd cycle gutted node_modules to ~39 packages, so `next build` failed on `Cannot find module '@tailwindcss/postcss'` (tailwind lives in devDeps). Fixes: plist now execs `/bin/bash /Users/parhar/bin/oscrsj-renderer-start.sh` (deployed copy outside the Documents tree; repo copy is canonical — redeploy via `cat` + `launchctl kickstart`), `WorkingDirectory` key dropped (script cds via absolute path), wrapper installs with `--include=dev`. Plist template now tracked at `scripts/com.oscrsj.renderer.plist` (Session 70 used-but-untracked class). Verified full cycle: `launchctl unload` → port free → `load` → production server in ~20s → dev-marker grep 0 → Funnel reachable. Residual quirk: `git pull` inside launchd context gets TCC-denied (non-fatal by design; node has Documents access, git doesn't).

**Phase 4 — bearer auth on `/api/publish/[id]` (`295ade2`).** Same gate shape as the preview endpoint (503 if secret unconfigured, 401 on mismatch), placed before UUID/body parsing so unauthorized calls touch nothing. `CleanupPane` receives the secret server-side from `page.tsx` as `publishAuthToken` prop → threaded into `PublishProgress` which attaches the Authorization header (never `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`); `publish-one.mjs` reuses the secret it already reads from `.env.local`; SmokeRunner unaffected (authToken optional). Negative tests against the Funnel: no header → 401, wrong token → 401. Residual exposure flagged: the secret rides the server-rendered `/render/[id]` page payload — anyone who can load that page can read it; acceptable for Funnel-only deployment.

**Phase 5 — C2 JATS consent dedupe (`e304e3a`).** Fix landed in the RENDERER's `jatsXml.ts`, NOT `lib/publish/synthesize.ts` as the §11 item suggested — synthesize.ts never sees body sections; body (`Pandoc(cleanedHtml)`) and back-matter (declarations) only merge inside `generateJatsXml`. Root cause of the duplicate: every real publish path sends non-empty cleanedHtml (pane HTML or `prep.rawHtml`), and `extractBody`'s SKIP_HEADINGS only filters `payload.body` (the PDF path) — so JATS `<body>` always kept the .docx consent section while `<back>` added the metadata one. New `stripBodySecsByTitle` (balanced `<sec>` scan, no non-greedy-regex early-cut risk) runs only when `declarations.patient_consent.statement` is non-empty. Verified through real Pandoc: consent-set → body 0 / back 1; consent-unset → body 1 / back 0; sibling sections intact. NOTE: e0001's *stored* JATS still carries the duplicate — needs a re-publish (Kanwar-gated).

**Phase 6 — Narrative Review backend (OSCRSJ `f925127` + renderer `3accc3b`).** Ships `^handoff-sushant-narrative-review-launch-2026-05-20`. COMBINED migration 025 (`025_narrative_review_and_checklist_file_types.sql`) adds `narrative_review` + `sanra_self_rating` + `prisma_checklist` in one Studio run (Phases 6+7 share ALTER TYPE mechanics — called out in both commit bodies). `ManuscriptType` + `FileType` unions extended; tsc then surfaced 7 exhaustive-Record errors across 6 files and a manual sweep covered the 10 non-exhaustive label maps (15 TYPE_LABELS surfaces total incl. the Session 66 `app/page.tsx` deferral). Step 1 radio at position 4; Step 2 `SANRA_SLOT` (required) + revision mirror (optional) + builder branches; wizard `computeInitialStep` + `step2Complete` gates; SERVER-side gate in `submitManuscript` (client gates are bypassable); `ABSTRACT_LABEL_SETS.narrative_review = [Background, Scope, Findings, Conclusion]`; synthesize.ts TYPE_DISPLAY/TYPE_SLUG/STRUCTURED_ABSTRACT_COUNT(4). Renderer: `'Narrative Review' → review-article` JATS mapping, explicit two-row collapse per Janine's indexing note. daily-digest needed NO edit (humanManuscriptType is now a generic snake_case→Title-Case converter — handoff line refs were stale). Deferred + named: build-script .docx blocks (Franklin owns content), Janine items 2-4 (all non-blocking).

**Phase 7 — PRISMA hard-gate (`9e9afd2`).** Closes the Session 50 §11 follow-up. `PRISMA_CHECKLIST_SLOT` (required, links existing `/downloads/oscrsj-prisma-checklist.pdf`) + revision mirror + builder branches for `review_article`; both wizard gates; server-side gate in `submitManuscript`; Step6Review label; migration 019 stale comment fixed (no longer lists review_article as checklist-free; defers to 025's superseding COMMENT); `/templates` "portal hard-gates forthcoming" copy → "hard gate on Submit for all four article types". No new migration file — rides combined 025.

### Session 76 — 2026-06-07 — Sushant Cowork (retroactive entry, backfilled Session 78) — Co-author dispute false-objection fix: GET renders confirm/object landing page, POST mutates — 1 OSCRSJ commit `c77ec51`  ^session-76-coauthor-false-objections

Retroactive narrative written during Session 78's wrap (the session landed its commit without an M1-M4 pass). The co-author notification email carried a single objection-only CTA whose link **mutated state on a bare GET** — two failure modes produced repeated phantom "co-author objected" reports: (1) co-authors who wanted to confirm clicked the only button available and filed objections against themselves; (2) email security scanners and link-preview bots (Outlook SafeLinks, Mimecast, Gmail) auto-prefetched the URL and filed objections with no human action — explaining several co-authors "objecting" at once. Commit `c77ec51` (2 files, +387/-121): GET `/api/submissions/[id]/co-author-dispute` now renders a read-only landing page with explicit Confirm / Object choices and performs no mutation; POST (`action=confirm|object`) does the actual change; error-page contact swapped from dead-letter `editorial@oscrsj.com` (no MX) to `oscrsjournal@gmail.com`; landing/result pages emit noindex,nofollow. Backward compatible — in-flight tokens open the safe page instead of auto-objecting.

**Context review.** Brad Agent.md read at session open. Entity Status section confirmed "Current entity: None — OSCRSJ has no legal entity yet." Crossref handoff (`^handoff-brad-crossref-membership-2026-05-16`) showed the full 7-step membership spec (Crossref membership → DOI prefix → JATS Crossref deposit → real DOI registration), gated on LLC formation. DOI placeholder currently `10.XXXXX/oscrsj.{year}.{padded-elocation}` in both the rendered PDF/A-1b and JATS XML. Dependency chain confirmed: LLC → EIN → Crossref membership (~$275/yr) → DOI prefix → retroactive registration of e0001 + automation for future articles.

**WA vs CA comparison.** Discussed entity-formation state options. WA chosen over CA: no state income tax (CA levies income tax), no franchise tax (CA charges $800/yr minimum even at $0 revenue — $800 for Year 1 alone regardless of APC revenue). WA Year 1 total: $200 filing + $70 annual report due by first anniversary = ~$270. Single-member LLC (not multi-member, not corporation) because OSCRSJ has one owner (Kanwar), no employees yet, and single-member LLCs are treated as "disregarded entities" by the IRS — pass-through to Schedule C, no double taxation, no corporate formalities. S-Corp discussion: paying a co-worker $35K/year from another job to justify S-Corp salary-split was discussed and ruled out — OSCRSJ has $0 revenue today and the payroll + accountant overhead ($1-2K/yr) outweighs the tax savings. Decision: S-Corp election is deferred; when net profit consistently exceeds $40-50K/yr, convert via IRS Form 2553 without dissolving the LLC.

**WA LLC formation — filed live.** Navigated sos.wa.gov CCFS system via screenshots. Key decisions made during the form:

- Entity type: Limited Liability Company (LLC)
- "Defer Initial Report" NOT checked — combined the Certificate of Formation + Initial Report into a single submission (saves a second filing)
- Nature of Business: PROFESSIONAL, SCIENTIFIC & TECHNICAL SERVICES (NAICS 54 — covers academic publishing / scientific journals)
- Registered Agent: "I am the Registered Agent" + "Non Commercial Registered Agent" — saves $100-150/yr vs a paid registered-agent service; Kanwar's address is the registered address
- Duration: Perpetual
- Effective date: Date of Filing (no reason to delay)
- Authorized Person Title: "Member" (correct LLC owner terminology, not "manager" unless manager-managed)
- Payment: VISA ••4654

TypeScript clean for both commits (`npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0). Pushed `c136f54..f5bc325`. Production curl post-deploy confirmed build ID `9thciyUXBqvOuGN516mzk` is serving.

**FUSE friction this session.** Three patterns surfaced and were worked around: (a) `mv /tmp/foo file` failed cross-device (tmp on sandbox-FS, file on FUSE-mount) — pivoted to `cat .tmpfile > original`; (b) `rm tmpfile` blocked by FUSE — pivoted to `mv tmpfile .git/.stale-junk/`; (c) `git/index.lock` blocked a `git add` until the proactive `mv .git/*.lock .git/.stale-junk/` sweep ran first. All three are covered by existing Conventions §3 or refinements proposed below.

**Self-improvement notes (proposed Convention §3 entries).** (1) **FUSE temp-file replacement pattern.** Working pattern for in-place file truncation/replacement: `head -n N file > .tmpfile && cat .tmpfile > file && mv .tmpfile .git/.stale-junk/`. The cross-device-rename trap (`/tmp` → FUSE) and the cannot-`rm`-FUSE-tmp trap both have known workarounds; documenting them in one place keeps future sessions from re-discovering. Worth folding into Convention §3 alongside the existing FUSE stale-lock workaround. (2) **Avoid placeholder-then-truncate Edit pattern.** Mid-session I added a deliberate `const __legacy_drop_below__ = \`` template-literal sentinel intending to truncate the trailing legacy block after writing the placeholder. Brittle: the file became syntactically invalid until the truncation landed, and the second-step truncation became its own micro-puzzle. Better pattern when trimming a large trailing block: read full content, then `Write` the file fresh with the trimmed version. One operation, no transient broken state. (3) **Server-side authority audit before scoping a UI-gate removal.** A 90-second grep of `submitManuscript` + `submitRevision` upfront confirmed neither validates suggested reviewers; the audit prevented an over-scoped commit that would have phantom-edited backend code. When removing a "required" constraint from a wizard step, grep every server action that touches the submitted entity to confirm the server doesn't independently enforce the same constraint.

**Vault note candidate (not touched this session).** The Submission Portal Architecture Plan at `02 - OSCRSJ/Projects/Submission Portal Architecture Plan.md` references the wizard step structure; should be updated to note the wizard is now 6 steps (added "Review & Submit") and the final-confirmation pattern lives in `Step6Review.tsx`. ### Session 72 — 2026-05-22 — Sushant Cowork — Remove main manuscript upload from submission wizard — 1 OSCRSJ commit `8cbb869`  ^session-72-remove-main-manuscript-upload

Kanwar request: "From the submission portal system, can you remove the main manuscript upload section. I don't think it is adding much value."

The "Main Manuscript" (`file_type='manuscript'`) slot was the unblinded copy of the manuscript — i.e., the full version with all author information. OSCRSJ's submission structure already requires (a) a **Blinded Manuscript** for peer review and (b) a **Title Page** carrying all author information as a separate file. The unblinded main manuscript was a third copy that duplicated information already split across those two files, adding friction for submitters without adding editorial value.

`app/dashboard/submit/SubmissionWizard.tsx` — Removed `hasMainManuscript` variable and its use in `step2Complete` (was `hasMainManuscript && hasBlindedManuscript && reportingChecklistOk`, now `hasBlindedManuscript && reportingChecklistOk`). Removed the `hasMain` check from `computeInitialStep` so the wizard auto-advances past Step 2 once the blinded manuscript (+ checklist if applicable) is present.

`lib/submission/actions.ts` — Removed the `manuscript` file-type server-side guard in `submitManuscript` (line ~710) and the equivalent guard in `submitRevision` (line ~1185-1187). Both functions now only require `blinded_manuscript` (initial) plus `blinded_manuscript` + `tracked_changes` + `response_to_reviewers` (revision).

### Session 71 — 2026-05-22 — John + Franklin Cowork — Article e0001 SEO: citation_* Highwire tags + MedicalScholarlyArticle JSON-LD + sitemap injection + GSC resubmission — 1 OSCRSJ commit `9f590f2`  ^session-71-article-seo-citation-tags-sitemap

Triggered by Kanwar: "What is the SEO status of our first manuscript. Is it discoverable everywhere it needs to be?" Loaded John (Search, Trust & Discoverability Director) and ran a full three-surface discoverability audit of the live article page for e0001 (Bilateral acute exertional compartment syndrome of the forearms in a sport climber — `f8596ce8-2424-4a47-8537-5af9e8a2f5cd`, published 2026-05-19).

**Audit methodology.** Fetched the live article page via `mcp__workspace__web_fetch`, ran a Python regex pass over the raw HTML to extract all meta tags, OG tags, Twitter card tags, and JSON-LD blocks. Fetched `sitemap.xml` and `robots.txt` in parallel. Read `app/articles/[id]/page.tsx` to understand what `generateMetadata()` was emitting. Fetched PDF proxy headers via `curl -sI`.

**Findings (4 🔴 critical, 4 🟡 attention, 8 🟢 passing).** Critical: (1) Zero `citation_*` Highwire Press meta tags — the only signal Google Scholar uses to decide whether to index a page; without them, Googlebot-Scholar can crawl the page forever and ignore it. (2) Article URL absent from sitemap.xml — 62 static URLs but zero individual article pages; Google discovers the article only via link crawl from `/articles`. (3) No `MedicalScholarlyArticle` JSON-LD — only site-level `WebSite + Organization + Periodical` graph from the layout; AI tools (Perplexity, ChatGPT, Gemini) parse JSON-LD for citable entities. (4) Google Scholar publisher notification never sent. Attention: keywords meta falling back to site-level boilerplate; Twitter card showing site name not article title; no OG image (blank social shares); PDF filename `oscrsj-manuscript-f8596ce8.pdf` (elocation_id null in DB). Passing: title tag, meta description (article-specific), canonical (www), robots index/follow, robots.txt (all bots allowed including Googlebot-Scholar, GPTBot, Claudebot), PDF publicly accessible with no auth and Cache-Control: public, OG title/description/url/type article-specific.

**Franklin implementation — commit `9f590f2` — 2 files, +114/-3 LoC.**

`app/articles/[id]/page.tsx` — `generateMetadata()` extended with full `citation_*` block via Next.js `other` field: `citation_title`, `citation_author` (one `<meta>` per author), `citation_journal_title` (full journal name), `citation_publisher`, `citation_volume` (1), `citation_issue` (1), `citation_publication_date` (YYYY/MM/DD), `citation_firstpage` (elocation_id when non-null), `citation_abstract` (full text), `citation_keyword` (one tag per keyword), `citation_pdf_url`, `citation_fulltext_html_url`. Added article-specific `keywords` field. Added `twitter` override (was showing site boilerplate). Added `MedicalScholarlyArticle` JSON-LD script block in page JSX body: `headline`, `url`, `datePublished`, `description`, `author[]` (Person nodes with affiliation + ORCID sameAs), `publisher → #organization`, `isPartOf → #periodical`, `isAccessibleForFree: true`, `license: CC BY 4.0`, `about: MedicalCondition → subspecialty`, `pagination` (elocation_id), `keywords`. Wrapped page return in `<>` fragment.

`app/sitemap.ts` — converted to `async function`, imported `createAdminClient`, added Supabase query for all `status='published'` manuscripts typed via `.returns<{ id: string; updated_at: string | null }[]>()`. Articles emitted at `priority: 0.9, changeFrequency: 'monthly'`. Failure-safe: Supabase unavailable at build time falls back to static pages without crashing. Article URLs prepended to the return array. TypeScript clean.

**Verification.** Production curl confirmed all tags live: `citation_title`, `citation_author` (two tags: Mantu Singh + Sharnjeet Bhardwaj), `citation_publication_date` (2026/05/21), `citation_pdf_url`, `citation_keyword` (three separate tags: Compartment Syndrome / Fasciotomy / Rhabdomyolysis), `twitter:title` (article title), `MedicalScholarlyArticle` (present), sitemap contains `f8596ce8` URL. Closes John §11 follow-up "Wire `/articles/[id]` dynamic URLs into sitemap" and "Submit sitemap to Google Search Console."

**GSC resubmission.** Confirmed Google Scholar has no manual notification form for custom journal websites — indexing is crawler-driven. Navigated GSC → Sitemaps via Chrome MCP. Sitemap previously submitted 2026-04-23, last read 2026-05-21, 68 discovered pages. Resubmitted `sitemap.xml` → "Sitemap submitted successfully," Submitted date updated to May 22, 2026.

Full narrative at [docs/session-history.md#session-71-article-seo-citation-tags-sitemap](docs/session-history.md). **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 70 — 2026-05-21 — Sushant Cowork — Cleanup-pane Publish button fix: force renderer production mode via launchd-start.sh + dev-mode warning banner — 1 renderer commit `6078130` + 1 OSCRSJ commit `9c3db3f`  ^session-70-cleanup-pane-publish-button-fix

Single sitting that diagnosed and shipped the fix for the Session 62 publication-arc follow-up "Diagnose cleanup pane Publish button UI bug." Kanwar's frustration: clicking the green "Publish manuscript…" button at the renderer's `/render/[id]` cleanup pane (accessed through `https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net/render/[id]`) produced no visible response — no modal, no error, no console output other than cosmetic HMR WebSocket failures. He'd been working around it for two sessions by running `scripts/publish-one.mjs <id>` from terminal (proven Session 62, used for article 1's re-publish in Session 69). User opened the session with "The publish manuscript button is not working when I click on render publish PDF in the submission portal system. I am having to manually do this through terminal. Figure out what is going on and solve it."

**Pre-flight (code-only diagnosis pass).** Loaded the renderer repo via `mcp__cowork__request_cowork_directory` (~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer was not in the initial mount set; only OSCRSJ + vault + Post Templates were). Read all 3 cleanup-pane files end-to-end: `app/render/[id]/CleanupPane.tsx` (215 LoC), `app/render/[id]/PublishProgress.tsx` (373 LoC), `app/render/[id]/page.tsx` (222 LoC). Click handler was canonical: `<button onClick={() => setPublishOpen(true)} disabled={!canPublish}>`. Modal positioning was `fixed inset-0 z-50 flex items-center justify-center bg-stone-950/60 p-6` — viewport-anchored, no parent transform/filter that would trap fixed positioning. Hydration mismatch from `useState(() => Date.now())` ruled out (only consumed inside the modal once published-open). The CLAUDE.md follow-up's stated hypothesis ("React Strict Mode double-mount edge case after `fb914ae`+`d44ef23` file additions confused Turbopack") was a red herring — code looked structurally correct.

**Critical divergence vs `publish-one.mjs`.** Cross-referenced the terminal workaround script (`scripts/publish-one.mjs`) against the renderer's `fetchSynthesizedPayload` in `page.tsx`. The script at line 75-78 handles both wrapped (`{ payload, warnings, errors }`) AND raw-payload response shapes: `payloadResponse?.payload && typeof payloadResponse.payload === 'object' ? payloadResponse.payload : payloadResponse`. The renderer's page.tsx at line 86 only handles wrapped: `payload: data.payload ?? null`. Hypothesis: if OSCRSJ's payload endpoint returned the raw payload, page.tsx would set payload=null, `canPublish=false`, button disabled. Tested by reading `app/api/publish/payload/[id]/route.ts` from OSCRSJ repo — endpoint returns `NextResponse.json({ ok: true, payload: result.payload, warnings: result.warnings, errors: [], manuscriptBodyCleanedHtml: bodyCleanedHtml })`. Wrapped shape confirmed. Hypothesis rejected; payload would deserialize correctly.

**Diagnostic curl that broke the case open.** Probed the live renderer through the Funnel: `curl -s https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net/`. The HTML response ended with `"b":"development"` (Next.js 16's runtime mode marker) and preloaded `[turbopack]_browser_dev_hmr-client_hmr-client_ts_*` plus `node_modules_next_dist_compiled_next-devtools_index_*`. **Diagnosis lock-in: the renderer was running `next dev` (Turbopack + HMR + next-devtools overlay), not `next start` (production).** Tailscale Funnel cannot proxy WebSocket connections, so the HMR client never completes its handshake. In Next.js 16 + React 19 + Turbopack dev mode, when HMR is broken, the next-devtools client fails to attach React event handlers to *some* client components — the textarea + preview iframe kept working (those handlers attached, that's why Kanwar didn't notice a global break), but the Publish button's onClick listener silently failed to bind. Click did nothing. This explains 100% of the observed symptoms: no console error (HMR failures are cosmetic from the user's perspective), other interactions working (selective handler-attachment failure), terminal workaround working (bypasses the button + modal layer entirely).

**Why production mode wasn't running despite the launchd plist's `npm start`.** Session 58 installed `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.oscrsj.renderer.plist` to auto-start the renderer in production at user login + KeepAlive on crash. Session 64's wrap-up noted that an existing manual `npm run dev` instance was winning the port-3001 race against the plist's `npm start`. The dev server (started during Kanwar's prior debugging) held port 3001; the plist's `npm start` would fail to bind, exit, KeepAlive would restart it, fail again, infinite loop. Net effect: dev server stayed up serving requests; production never ran. The CLAUDE.md §11 follow-up "Verify launchd auto-start on next Mac reboot" was effectively unresolved.

**Renderer commit `6078130` — 2 files, +99/-0.**

**(1) `scripts/launchd-start.sh` (NEW — created Session 58, never committed, ~3.4 KB):** patched the wrapper to evict stale processes BEFORE starting production. Before `npm install && npm run build && exec npm start`: `lsof -ti :3001` lists any PIDs bound to port 3001 (empty result swallowed via `|| true`); `kill -9` displaces them; `sleep 1` lets the OS release the port binding; belt-and-suspenders `pkill -9 -f "next dev"` catches any dev-server bound to a different port. Heavy-handed but the whole point is to guarantee the plist always wins. Code-comment block in-script documents the entire root-cause chain so the next session that touches this file has context. This file existed on disk since Session 58 but was never tracked — the §11 follow-up "Verify launchd auto-start" was therefore reading from working-tree state, not git state. Today's commit makes the script git-tracked.

**(2) `app/render/[id]/CleanupPane.tsx` (modified — added 25 LoC banner):** red dev-mode warning at the top of the cleanup pane when `process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production'`. Banner copy includes the exact `launchctl unload && launchctl load` reload command + the `node scripts/publish-one.mjs <id>` workaround link + a callout that the Publish button may not work behind Tailscale Funnel because Funnel cannot proxy WSS. If this regresses again it's diagnosable at-a-glance without curl-poking the HTML for `"b":"development"`. Banner only renders when NODE_ENV is non-production — production-built renderer never shows it.

**Cross-repo bookkeeping — OSCRSJ commit `9c3db3f`.** Two updates bundled into the OSCRSJ-side commit per the M1-M4 protocol: (a) CLAUDE.md §11 — marked the Session 62 follow-up `Diagnose cleanup pane Publish button UI bug` as ✅ done with full provenance + diagnosis chain, added 3 new follow-ups (push renderer commit, activate fix on Mac, visual spot-check the dev-mode banner). (b) CLAUDE.md §9 + docs/session-history.md — bundled the Session 67 narrative orphan (Session 67's `8a55c08` + `6218853` + `4006ab6` + `a77920d` landed in git but the M1-M4 narrative was left as uncommitted working-tree content). Per Convention §6 working-tree-orphan refinement, bundling with explicit attribution is the approved path when the orphan describes work that already landed.

**Activation cycle on Kanwar's Mac — five rounds of escalation before the renderer was fully on production.** (1) Pushed Mac-terminal command block; comments interpreted as commands (`zsh: command not found: #`) but actual commands ran. `git push origin main` returned "Everything up-to-date" — the renderer commit had landed on origin via some path I couldn't fully trace from sandbox (likely Kanwar's earlier interactive push). (2) Curled the Funnel URL post-launchctl-reload — STILL dev mode. `pkill -9 -f 'next dev'` killed the original next-server but the dev server got restarted (VS Code auto-restart task, manual relaunch, or pkill pattern miss). (3) Issued a broader diagnostic block: `lsof -nP -iTCP:3001 -sTCP:LISTEN`, `launchctl list | grep oscrsj`, `ps aux | grep -E 'next|node'`, broader pkill patterns (`next-server`, `next dev`, `turbopack`, `next start`), sleep 2, recheck port, launchctl unload + load, sleep 45, recheck, tail the launchd stderr.log. (4) Kanwar's output revealed two findings: (a) PID 57177 was `next-server (v16.2.4)` reading from `/Users/parhar/Documents/oscrsj-renderer/.next/dev/build/postcss.js` — definitively dev mode; (b) **after pkill cleared the port (`PORT_FREE`) and launchctl tried to re-load the plist, the plist's wrapper script failed with `/bin/bash: /Users/parhar/Documents/oscrsj-renderer/scripts/launchd-start.sh: Operation not permitted` repeated dozens of times** + `shell-init: error retrieving current directory: getcwd: cannot access parent directories: Operation not permitted` on every KeepAlive restart attempt. macOS was refusing to execute the script. (5) Pivoted to a launchd-bypass workaround: `launchctl unload ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.oscrsj.renderer.plist 2>/dev/null && cd ~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer && chmod +x scripts/launchd-start.sh && xattr -c scripts/launchd-start.sh && npm run build && npm start`. Build completed cleanly in 1912ms + TypeScript in 1346ms + page generation in 305ms + static pages in 58ms; `next start -p 3001` started production server in 70ms. Post-fix curl returned zero `hmr-client`, zero `next-devtools`, zero `"b":"development"` markers — production confirmed.

**Why `launchd-start.sh` won't execute under launchd.** Root cause hypothesis (not fully proven this session): the file was created via the Cowork sandbox's FUSE mount, owned at the sandbox-FS level by `vibrant-clever-rubin:vibrant-clever-rubin` (the sandbox user). When macOS-side launchd tries to exec the script, the FUSE filesystem's ownership-remapping interacts with launchd's stricter Documents-folder TCC restrictions (macOS Sequoia/Tahoe progressively tightened launchd's access to user data directories). Manual execution from Terminal works because Terminal has Full Disk Access or because Terminal runs as the parhar user with direct permission. launchd's exec context differs. Workaround for now: Kanwar runs `npm run build && npm start` in a Terminal tab manually post-reboot. Permanent fix paths (filed as §11 follow-ups): copy the script outside the FUSE mount to `~/bin/oscrsj-renderer-start.sh`, OR grant launchd Full Disk Access, OR use a different invocation pattern (e.g., `bash -c "..."` inline in the plist).

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0 before commit (renderer). Tree size check passed. Explicit-path stage per Convention §1. FUSE stale-lock proactive sweep per Convention §3 (warnings during commit + push but operations completed cleanly per Sessions 32 + 59 + 62 precedent). Post-fix curl through Funnel returned production-mode HTML. Kanwar to test the Publish button on the next manuscript publish.

**Risks named.** (1) **Renderer dies when Terminal tab closes.** Foreground `npm start` means closing the tab or rebooting kills the renderer. Acceptable until the launchd permission issue is fixed; Kanwar reruns the command when needed. (2) **The launchd plist is in an unloaded state.** Future reboots won't auto-start the renderer until either (a) the launchd permission issue is fixed and the plist is re-loaded, or (b) Kanwar adds the npm start command to login items / a different auto-start mechanism. (3) **VS Code might still auto-run `npm run dev`.** If Kanwar opens the renderer in VS Code and a `.vscode/tasks.json` or terminal restoration auto-launches dev mode, port 3001 gets re-claimed. Not investigated this session. (4) **The Publish button fix is unverified end-to-end** — Kanwar will try on the next manuscript publish. If it still doesn't work, dig further (possibly the production-mode bundle has its own Funnel-WSS issue, though that's unlikely). (5) **No path forward yet on launchd permission**: filed as §11 follow-ups for next Sushant session.

**Self-improvement notes.** (1) **`curl the URL and grep for `"b":"development"` is the first diagnostic step for any Funnel-served renderer/Vercel UI button bug.** Saved hours of speculative debugging this session. The CLAUDE.md follow-up's hypothesis blamed React Strict Mode + Turbopack file-confusion; the actual answer was visible in one HTTP response. Codify in Conventions: when any UI button on a Funnel-served or Vercel-served page mysteriously stops responding, first action is `curl -s <url> | grep -oE '"b":"production"|"b":"development"|hmr-client|next-devtools'`. (2) **Working-tree files surviving across sessions are an under-noticed bug class.** `scripts/launchd-start.sh` was created Session 58, used in production via launchd plist, BUT never tracked in git. Anyone with a fresh clone would have a broken launchd plist (file missing). Convention §6 covers the orphan-staged-files case; should extend to the orphan-untracked-but-used case. (3) **Convention §6 working-tree-orphan refinement was the right call** for the Session 67 narrative bundle — the feature commits were already in HEAD, the orphan was just documentation. Bundling with attribution closes the loop. (4) **Comments in pasted shell command blocks** get interpreted by zsh as `command not found: #`. Mostly harmless but noisy; the `~30s` expansion bug (zsh treats `~30s` as user-home expansion → no-such-user error) was a real issue. Future Mac-terminal command blocks: strip comments before pasting, OR put them on lines that start with `#` AT COLUMN ZERO (zsh does treat those as comments). Comment-with-leading-whitespace is the foot-gun.

**Kanwar follow-ups (immediate).** (1) Test Publish button on next manuscript publish — load `https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net/render/<some-id>`, click green "Publish manuscript…" button, confirm modal opens with confirmation checkbox + chain stages list; checking the box should start streaming NDJSON events through the chain. (2) If next publish is via a fresh Mac reboot, Kanwar needs to re-run `cd ~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer && npm run build && npm start` in a Terminal tab before the publish will work — the launchd plist is unloaded. (3) Visual spot-check the dev-mode warning banner: in a test session, run `npm run dev -p 3001` instead of `npm start`, reload the cleanup pane, confirm the red banner renders with the launchctl reload command + publish-one.mjs workaround. Kill the dev server when done. (4) The launchd permission issue is filed as §11 follow-ups for a future Sushant session — three plausible fix paths sketched (copy script outside FUSE, grant launchd Full Disk Access, inline-bash in plist).

**Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 69 — 2026-05-21 — Sushant Cowork — C1/C3 post-publish hotfixes (handling editor name + verapdf false-positive) + article 1 re-publish — 1 renderer commit `8f1888b`  ^session-69-c1-c3-hotfixes-republish

Single Sushant Cowork sitting that closed the two remaining 🔴 critical items from Janine's first-publish compliance audit (Session 68). Both were renderer/data-layer defects identified in the JATS XML and render-report artifacts for article e0001. C2 (contradictory consent) had already been resolved by Kanwar in Session 68 via the admin metadata editor.

**C1 — Handling editor name stored as email address.** The JATS `<contrib contrib-type="editor">` block was emitting `<surname>kanwarparhar@gmail.com</surname>` with empty `<given-names>`. Root cause: NOT a code bug — `lib/publish/synthesize.ts` correctly reads `users.full_name`, but Kanwar's admin row in the Supabase `users` table had the email address in the `full_name` column (the row was auto-created during auth signup without a profile completion step). Fix: direct SQL update via Supabase Studio — `UPDATE users SET full_name = 'Kanwar Parhar' WHERE email = 'kanwarparhar@gmail.com'`. Confirmed via SELECT. No code change required. The synthesizer code is correct; only the data row was wrong.

**C3 — verapdf parser false-positive.** The `render-report.json` was recording `verapdf.result: "pass"` despite `rawOutput` containing `FAIL /var/.../archive.pdf 1b`. Root cause: `lib/renderer/verapdf.ts` `parseVerapdfTextOutput()` had `overallLine` detection regex `/^(PASS|FAIL)\s+PDF\/A-1B/i`. This matched the OLD verapdf CLI format where the flavour name followed immediately (`FAIL PDF/A-1B`). verapdf 1.30.1 batch mode changed the output format to `FAIL /path/to/file.pdf 1b` (file path then flavour level). The regex never matched any line → fell to the fallback heuristic `hasRules ? 'fail' : 'pass'` → zero Rule lines on a compliant PDF → silently returns `'pass'` regardless of what verapdf actually reported. Fix: broaden the `overallLine` regex from `/^(PASS|FAIL)\s+PDF\/A-1B/i` to `/^(PASS|FAIL)\s+/i` — captures the status keyword regardless of what follows. One character range change. Renderer commit `8f1888b`: `fix(verapdf): broaden overallLine regex to match verapdf 1.30.1 batch output format`. Pushed to `origin/main` by Kanwar.

**Re-publish.** After Kanwar pushed `8f1888b`, article 1 was re-published via `node scripts/publish-one.mjs f8596ce8-2424-4a47-8537-5af9e8a2f5cd 2>&1`. The new render cycle baked in both fixes: JATS XML now carries `<surname>Kanwar Parhar</surname><given-names></given-names>` in the handling editor block, and the verapdf result now correctly reflects the real PDF/A-1b determination from the CLI output.

**Session wrap-up.** Compliance audit note updated — all three 🔴 items closed. CLAUDE.md §9/§11 and docs/session-history.md updated per M1-M4 protocol.

**Verification.** `8f1888b` — single-line diff, regressed verapdf output format documented in commit message with the old vs. new format strings. Supabase SELECT confirmed `full_name = 'Kanwar Parhar'` post-UPDATE.

**Risks named.** (1) **verapdf real result on article 1 PDF unknown until next publish.** The fix corrects the parser; the next re-publish will reveal whether the stored PDF genuinely passes or fails PDF/A-1b. If it fails, WeasyPrint PDF/A-1b output will need investigation. (2) **Supabase `full_name` column is the single point of truth for JATS editor name** — if other admin users are invited in the future and their profile rows similarly lack `full_name`, the same C1 defect will recur. Mitigation: add a Sushant follow-up to add `full_name` validation to the admin-user invitation flow (low priority). (3) **No renderer smoke test ran this session** — the fix is a one-line regex change with obvious semantics, but the 3-fixture smoke battery wasn't exercised. Carries a small regression risk; follow-up smoke is recommended.

**Kanwar follow-ups (immediate).** (1) After next publish, check render-report.json's `verapdf.result` field to confirm it now reflects the real verapdf determination. (2) Optionally run the 3 renderer smoke fixtures to confirm the regex change doesn't break anything: `npm run dev` → `/smoke/sample-payload`, `/smoke/sample-payload-8-authors`, `/smoke/sample-payload-reused-figure`. Full narrative at [docs/session-history.md#session-69-c1-c3-hotfixes-republish](docs/session-history.md). **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 68 — 2026-05-21 — Janine Cowork — First-publish compliance audit of e0001 + /faq CC BY-NC-ND → CC BY 4.0 fix + ISSN Application Prep vault file  ^session-68-janine-first-publish-compliance-audit

Single Janine Cowork sitting triggered by the `^handoff-janine-first-publish-compliance-review-2026-05-16` handoff, which fired on 2026-05-19 when article 1 (e0001, "Bilateral acute exertional compartment syndrome of the forearms in a sport climber: a case report") went live. Audit covered the PDF (3.68 MB, 7 pages), JATS XML (38 KB), and render-report.json (5.2 KB) produced by the Session 62 publication arc.

**Audit findings — 3 tiers.**

*Tier 1 🔴 Critical (3 items, all requiring re-publish):*

(C1) **Handling editor JATS block carries email address as `<surname>`.** `<surname>kanwarparhar@gmail.com</surname>` — synthesizer correctly reads `users.full_name`, but Kanwar's admin Supabase row stored the email in that column. PMC ingest would fail. Root cause: data, not code.

(C2) **Contradictory patient consent statements.** Article body contained an `adult_living` consent statement ("Written informed consent was obtained...") from the .docx body, while the back-matter declaration block used `deidentified_no_consent_required` ("The case is fully de-identified..."). These are directly contradictory — an ICMJE compliance violation. Root cause: the metadata editor had the wrong variant selected; the synthesizer emits both body-extracted AND declaration-block consent sections without deduplication.

(C3) **verapdf parser false-positive.** `render-report.json verapdf.result: "pass"` despite `rawOutput: "FAIL /var/.../archive.pdf 1b"`. The parser regex `/^(PASS|FAIL)\s+PDF\/A-1B/i` didn't match verapdf 1.30.1's new output format. Fell to heuristic → false pass.

*Tier 2 🟡 Attention (3 items):* No ORCID for either author (now a warning, not error, per CEO override 2026-05-17) · DTD validation never ran (known §11 open follow-up) · Placeholder DOI (expected; gated on Crossref/Brad's LLC lane).

*Tier 3 🟢 Clean (13 checks):* XMP `xmpRights:WebStatement` CC BY 4.0 URL, JATS `<license-p>` + `ali:license_ref`, article-type mapping, 4-section structured abstract, keywords 3–5, title no trailing period, 21-entry ref-list, AI disclosure present and explicitly negative, back-matter 7 required sections, pub-date JATS attributes, page count, wallclock render time, synthesizer sanity tests 10/10 — all clean.

### Session 67 — 2026-05-21 — Sushant Cowork — Public article display pipeline: PDF proxy + figure proxy + article detail page + homepage figures + in-press status fix — 3 OSCRSJ commits `8a55c08` + `4006ab6` + `a77920d`  ^session-67-article-display-pipeline

Single Sushant Cowork sitting that built the full public-facing article display pipeline, triggered by Kanwar observing the published article showing "Full text available soon" with no way to read it, no individual article page, and the homepage still rendering its empty-state despite the article being live.

**Session start diagnostics.** Two immediate action items identified from §11: (1) push renderer commits `d44ef23` + `4b49660` that were Mac-only and at risk of being lost on reboot. Computer-use tools loaded; Terminal is click-only tier (no typing), so command written to clipboard via `write_clipboard` and Kanwar pasted + confirmed push (`d44ef23..4b49660  main -> main`). Both renderer commits now safely on `origin/main`. (2) Article page audit: `/articles` showed the card with "Full text available soon" hardcoded; `/articles/in-press` showed empty ("No Articles in Press Yet"). Root cause: `BrowserArticle` type had no `pdfStoragePath` field, the listing rendered the text unconditionally, `in-press` queried only `status='in_production'` (article went `accepted` → `published` directly), and no individual article page existed.

**Five display gaps audited and closed:**

1. **"Full text available soon" — hardcoded text, no PDF link.** `BrowserArticle` type had no `pdfStoragePath`. `loadPublishedArticles()` never passed `published_pdf_storage_path` through. Submissions bucket is private (signed URLs used throughout codebase) so a public endpoint was needed. Fix: new route `/api/articles/[id]/pdf` — public GET, `status='published'` gate, admin client for private-bucket access, streams PDF bytes with `Content-Disposition: inline`, filename derived from `elocation_id` (→ `oscrsj-e0001.pdf`), 1h CDN cache. `BrowserArticle` gains `pdfStoragePath`; article card footer conditionally renders "Full Text (PDF) ↓" link or the italic fallback. Commit `8a55c08`.

2. **No individual article page.** No `/articles/[id]` route existed anywhere. Built `app/articles/[id]/page.tsx` (~430 LoC) as a server component. Shows: dark hero header with article type + subspecialty chips, title, authors line (corresponding author starred in peach, with degrees), affiliations numbered list, Volume/Issue/eLocation identifiers, DOI or "pending", peach Download PDF button. Body: Abstract section (plain text with keyword row), Declarations section (COI, funding, data availability, AI tools from `manuscript_metadata`). Sidebar: Citation string (Vancouver-style, select-all mono block), CC BY 4.0 license card with link, corresponding author card (name, affiliation, ORCID), sidebar PDF download button, ← All Articles back link. `generateMetadata` emits title, description (abstract truncated to 200 chars), canonical URL, OG article type with author array. 404 for any non-published manuscript ID. Commit `4006ab6`.

3. **Article titles unlinked.** `/articles` listing: article `<h2>` wrapped in `<Link href=/articles/[id]>` with `group-hover:text-ink` transition. Homepage cards: title `<h3>` wrapped in Link + "Read article" button changed from DOI-gated `<a href=doi.org/...>` to always-visible `<Link href=/articles/[id]>`. Commit `4006ab6`.

4. **In-press never triggered.** Page queried `eq('status', 'in_production')`. Article went `accepted` → `published` via `publish-one.mjs`, skipping `in_production` entirely — so it never surfaced on the in-press page. Fix: changed both the count query (for `generateMetadata` noindex logic) and the main fetch to `in(['accepted', 'in_production'])`. Future articles will appear in In Press as soon as they are accepted. Commit `4006ab6`.

5. **Homepage figure placeholder never replaced.** Homepage article card showed a cream/taupe CSS gradient with "Radiograph" text. Built new public proxy route `/api/articles/[id]/figure` — same security pattern as PDF proxy, fetches `manuscript_files` row with `file_type='figure'` and `file_order ASC LIMIT 1`, downloads from submissions bucket, infers Content-Type from extension (jpeg/png/gif/webp/tiff/svg), returns image bytes with 1h CDN cache. Since the homepage is a server component and `onError` is a browser event handler that can't live in RSC JSX, created `components/ArticleFigureImage.tsx` as a `'use client'` island: renders `<img src=/api/articles/[id]/figure>` with a `useState` fallback to the gradient when `onError` fires (404 from proxy = no figure). Homepage card figure div replaced with `<ArticleFigureImage>`. Figure tile is also wrapped in a `<Link>` to the article detail page. Commit `a77920d`.

**File-level changes across 3 commits (+606 LoC net):**
- `app/api/articles/[id]/pdf/route.ts` — new (85 LoC)
- `app/api/articles/[id]/figure/route.ts` — new (87 LoC)
- `app/articles/[id]/page.tsx` — new (434 LoC)
- `components/ArticleFigureImage.tsx` — new (46 LoC)
- `app/articles/ArticlesBrowser.tsx` — modified (pdfStoragePath field + title Link + PDF conditional)
- `app/articles/in-press/page.tsx` — modified (status filter expanded)
- `app/page.tsx` — modified (FeaturedArticle type + ArticleFigureImage + Link titles + unconditional Read article)

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0 after each commit. Tree size 301 → 302 → 303 → 305 (3 new routes + 1 component added across 3 commits). Explicit-path stage for all commits; `app/faq/page.tsx` orphan identified as Janine's CC BY-NC-ND fix and left unstaged per Convention §6. FUSE stale-lock sweep (`mv .git/*.lock`) before each commit.

**Cross-session orphan noted.** `app/faq/page.tsx` has Janine's CC BY-NC-ND → CC BY 4.0 fix for two FAQ Q&As already applied to the working tree but never committed. Good change, belongs in Janine's commit. Filed in §11.

**Risks named.** (1) Figure proxy returns 404 if no `manuscript_files` row with `file_type='figure'` exists for the article — `ArticleFigureImage` fallback handles this gracefully. Kanwar should verify figure rows exist in Supabase Studio → manuscript_files for `f8596ce8`. (2) Article detail page shows abstract as a single `whitespace-pre-line` block — structured sub-sections (Background/Case/Discussion/Conclusion) from the PDF are not broken out. Acceptable for MVP; a future enhancement would parse the structured abstract. (3) Homepage card "Radiograph" placeholder copy was changed to "Figure" in the fallback — minor cosmetic. (4) No visual spot-check from sandbox post-deploy; filed as §11 follow-up.

**Kanwar follow-ups.** (1) Hard-refresh `/articles`, `/articles/[uuid]`, and homepage post-Vercel-deploy (~60s). (2) Verify figure appears on homepage card (if gradient shows, check Supabase Studio `manuscript_files` for figure rows). (3) Verify `/articles/in-press` now shows the article on the next accepted manuscript. Full narrative here. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

Kanwar's ask, framed as a downstream resume of Manvir's handoff: "Franklin, work on the narrative review addition information that was pushed to you" — pointed at `^handoff-franklin-narrative-review-launch-2026-05-20` filed earlier same-day by a Manvir Cowork session after the Tbilisi State rotator-cuff manuscript surfaced the category gap. The handoff was sized at ~3 Cowork sessions total (site copy + .docx template + worked example); this session closed the site-copy sweep. Two follow-up sessions remain (.docx template + .docx worked example; both blocked on Janine's SANRA PDF + clinical-topic coordination).

**Pre-flight via AskUserQuestion (2 questions).** (a) Scope decision — full 3-Cowork-session combine, site-copy-only, or site-copy + empty template? Kanwar picked the recommended **site copy sweep only**. Rationale: Janine's SANRA self-rating PDF (the only declared upstream blocker) hadn't shipped yet, so the .docx worked-example also needed Janine's clinical-topic input first; splitting the work into one coherent commit per concern keeps each commit small and bisectable. (b) CC BY-NC-ND fix scope — bundle just the /templates surface (per handoff spec) or close out the open §11 follow-up by also flipping the two /faq Q&As? Kanwar picked the recommended **just /templates** to keep agent-lane boundaries clean (the FAQ Q&As remain in Janine's lane on her open follow-up). Pre-emptive AskUserQuestion saved ~30 minutes — without it I would have either over-scoped to all 3 sessions or guessed wrong on the CC BY-NC-ND scope.

**Cross-agent dependency caught during scope read.** `app/page.tsx` is listed as a deliverable in the Franklin handoff (item 4: add `narrative_review: 'Narrative Review'` to the `TYPE_LABELS` constant at lines 46-53). But the constant is typed `Record<ManuscriptType, string>` where `ManuscriptType` is a union in `lib/types/database.ts` (line 12). Adding a key without extending the union breaks tsc with a missing-key error; extending the union without the matching migration would either break tsc on the ~12 other `Record<ManuscriptType, ...>` consumers in the codebase (admin pages, email templates, daily digest, articles listing, reviewer pages, submission wizard) OR ship a type union that drifts from the Postgres enum (which would runtime-fail any write of `manuscript_type='narrative_review'` until Sushant's migration 025 lands). The Sushant handoff explicitly owns "10+ TYPE_LABELS surface updates" + "lib/types/database.ts type union extensions." This is real handoff over-spec on Franklin's side. **Resolution:** defer the `app/page.tsx` TYPE_LABELS entry to Sushant's migration 025 commit (where the type union extension belongs as a coherent set), document the deferral in the commit message + agent-file Outbound, and file as a §11 follow-up so neither agent forgets. Net: 4 files edited this session instead of 5.

**Repo-state pre-flight per Convention §1.** HEAD started at `4d5113a` ("docs(claude-md): M1-M4 wrap-up for Session 65"); tree size 301 (healthy). Working tree clean except expected tooling (`.claude/worktrees/`, `.cowork-push/`). No orphan working-tree files this session. Proactive FUSE stale-lock sweep before each git op per Convention §3.

Both Manvir-authored same session. Caught at pre-flight via direct grep + type-system reading; no commit damage. Codification candidate (§3) below. (5) **No Rich Results Test of FAQ JSON-LD** — the new Q4 on `/guide-for-authors` is structurally identical to the existing three Q&As (which validate clean), but FAQ JSON-LD has been broken before by quote-escape bugs. Confirmed scope decision via AskUserQuestion.

**Self-improvement notes.** (1) **AskUserQuestion pre-flight saved ~30 minutes of scope drift.** The 3-Cowork-session handoff size made it tempting to either over-scope (one giant commit with placeholder content) or under-scope (just `/aims-scope`). Asking explicitly "how much in this session?" + offering a recommended option with reasoning got a clean lock in one round. Same pattern applied to the CC BY-NC-ND scope decision. Codification: when an inbound handoff is sized for multiple sessions, pre-flight always asks how much of it to bite off; never assume "all of it" without confirming. (2) **Type-system check before constant-table edits.** The `app/page.tsx` TYPE_LABELS deliverable looked like a 1-line edit until I checked the constant's type signature — `Record<ManuscriptType, string>` immediately revealed the cross-agent lane crossing. Codification candidate for Conventions §3: "Before adding to any `Record<UnionType, ...>` constant, grep for the union definition and verify whether extending the union is in your agent lane. If the union extension belongs to another agent's migration commit, defer the constant entry to that commit; don't ship a half-migration." (3) **Handoff over-spec detection via grep.** Two agents specing the same file (`app/page.tsx` TYPE_LABELS in both Franklin's and Sushant's handoffs, both authored same session) is a real risk class. Codification candidate: at scope-read time, grep the other agents' Inbound Handoffs for any file you're about to edit — catches double-spec before it ships as duplicated work or merge conflict. (4) **Verbose commit message that names every deferral** — three explicit deferrals (app/page.tsx TYPE_LABELS, .docx template, .docx example) named in the commit message body so that 6 months from now Kanwar (or another agent grepping git history) sees what wasn't done and why. Pattern worth codifying: commit messages for partial-spec sessions name the deferrals.

**Kanwar follow-ups (immediate).** (1) **Visual spot-check post-Vercel-deploy** (~60s after `d1207bc`) — verify (a) `/aims-scope` 7-item bullet list with Narrative Review at position 4, lead paragraph "seven article types"; (b) `/guide-for-authors` new Narrative Review article-type detail section between SR/MA and Surgical Technique with SANRA checklist card visible (the download button itself will 404 until Janine ships — that's expected); comparison table 7 columns no horizontal overflow on narrow mobile; new FAQ Q4 in JSON-LD; ethics section last bullet mentions SANRA; (c) `/templates` Narrative Review row in article-types section; 4-card SANRA reporting-checklists grid at desktop (2x2 at tablet, single-column at mobile); SANRA card download 404 expected; submissionFiles Reporting Checklist entry updated; Publication License section says CC BY 4.0; (d) `/faq` Q1 "seven article types", two new Q&As immediately after Q1, checklist Q includes SANRA, word-limit Q includes Narrative Review 4,000 words. (2) **Rich Results Test on `/guide-for-authors` + `/faq` JSON-LD** at https://search.google.com/test/rich-results — both pages have FAQ JSON-LD that may have a quote-escape sensitivity. (3) **Sushant follow-up** for homepage TYPE_LABELS bundled with migration 025 (filed §11). (4) **Janine SANRA PDF blocker** flagged for status check on her handoff (`^handoff-janine-narrative-review-launch-2026-05-20`).
