### Session 58 — 2026-05-16/17 — Sushant Cowork — Phase 1.5 docx-extract pre-fills shipped + Tailscale Funnel deployed (Vercel→localhost architectural reach gap fixed); 1 renderer commit + 1 OSCRSJ commit via PR #4; first-publish smoke partially executed on live oscrsj.com (extraction verified end-to-end)  ^session-58-docx-extract-tailscale-funnel

Two interlocking ships in one session: (a) the Phase 1.5 docx-extract feature that Sushant self-handed off at the end of Session 57 (Pandoc-based pre-fill from accepted manuscript .docx with regex/heuristic confidence scoring), and (b) the Tailscale Funnel infrastructure layer that fixed a previously-latent architectural gap — Vercel server functions calling `http://localhost:3001` were never going to reach Kanwar's Mac. Both Session 54's `/api/preview` and Session 57's `/api/preview/[id]` proxy had the same broken assumption; today's docx-extract proxy would have inherited it. The fix happens at deployment infrastructure level, not in code. End-state: editor pre-fills work end-to-end on live `oscrsj.com` against manuscript `f8596ce8-2424-4a47-8537-5af9e8a2f5cd`; full abstract sub-sections + Discussion populate with HIGH-confidence chips. First-publish smoke (running-title fix + Save + Preview + render + go-live) is now unblocked but defers to Kanwar's next sitting — out of scope for tonight.

#### Phase 1.5.A — Renderer extract endpoint + heuristic module (renderer commit `81d7985`)

New `lib/renderer/extractMetadata.ts` (~702 lines) + new `app/api/extract-metadata/[id]/route.ts` (~91 lines). Pandoc converts the latest accepted manuscript .docx to HTML5 (preferring `file_type='manuscript'`, falling back to `'blinded_manuscript'`); regex walks both **native** `<h1>`/`<h2>`/`<h3>`/`<h4>` AND **bold-leading-paragraph** `<p><strong>Label:</strong>` patterns (the more common Pandoc emission for authors who just bold the section label rather than apply Word Heading styles). Six metadata regions extracted: abstract sub-sections (sliced from Abstract heading to Keywords heading), conflict of interest, funding, IRB protocol number, patient consent (with Janine §3 7-variant guesser via distinctive-phrase match + best-effort institution + protocol extraction), and acknowledgments. Per-extraction confidence buckets: **HIGH** (≥0.7) auto-applies with chip "Pre-filled from .docx (HIGH)" + × dismiss; **AMBER** (0.4-0.7) pre-fills with "verify" chip; **LOW** (<0.4) leaves field empty + surfaces notice; **none** if no anchor matched. Score inputs (additive, clamped [0,1]): +0.5 baseline if heading anchor matched, +0.2 if native heading (vs bold paragraph), +0.2 if text length in expected range, −0.3 if suspiciously short (<20 chars), −0.2 if suspiciously long (>3000 chars). Endpoint architecture mirrors Session 57's `/api/preview/[id]`: POST + Bearer `${RENDERER_SHARED_SECRET}` auth + UUID sanity check; returns JSON (NOT NDJSON — extraction is fast enough to fit in one response, typically 1-3s). Source `file_type` returned so OSCRSJ audit-log records which variant produced the extraction. NO LLM-assist this phase per the self-handoff decision; regex-first with manual fallback. **NO publishChain.ts changes** — standalone extractor that runs independently. NO Storage writes. TypeScript clean exit 0 on renderer (`npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`). One ES2017-target nit caught in-session: the `s` flag (dotAll) isn't allowed under renderer's `target: ES2017` tsconfig; rewrote 4 regex patterns to use `[\s\S]` instead of `.` with `s` flag. Renderer tree size 54 → 56. Pushed to `origin/main` via Mac terminal at end of phase (`48d70f1..81d7985`).

#### Phase 1.5.B — OSCRSJ-side wiring + chips + divergence banners (OSCRSJ commit `b0c0cf7`, merged via PR #4 as `83eebe8`)

Four files / 999 insertions on OSCRSJ side. New `lib/publish/extractedMetadata.ts` (62 lines) — shared types mirroring renderer's `ExtractedFields` shape (separate file because renderer + OSCRSJ are independent tsconfig.json apps; runtime drift caught at route handler body parsing). New `app/api/extract-metadata/[id]/route.ts` (182 lines) — OSCRSJ proxy endpoint (POST, `requireAdminOnly`, attaches Bearer to renderer fetch). Best-effort audit-log row `metadata_extracted_from_docx` with confidence-summary per field. Returns 200 on graceful "no .docx available" — we don't want to break the editor page if extraction fails. **`MetadataEditorPanel.tsx`** (+97 lines) — server-side direct fetch of renderer's extract endpoint on first page-load (parallel with the existing synthesizer dry-run via `Promise.all`); graceful failure (`extract_error` surfaces as top banner; editor still loads with DB-only pre-fills); 90-second AbortSignal timeout for larger .docx files. Audit-log row written server-side too. **`MetadataEditorForm.tsx`** (+621 lines) — adds `decisions: InitialDecisions` state + `computeInitialDecisions(initial)` merge logic implementing a 4-state per-field decision system: `extracted_applied` (DB empty → extraction wins → HIGH/AMBER chip with × dismiss); `db_kept_diverges` (DB ≠ extraction → DB stays + yellow banner with `[Use .docx]` / `[Keep DB]` buttons); `db_matches_extraction` (values agree, no UI noise); `no_extraction` / `dismissed` (no UI). Loose-match (80% substring overlap on case+whitespace-normalized values) avoids false-divergence banners when "DB has 80% of paragraph; .docx has full paragraph with one extra sentence." Auto-application sets `isDirty` on mount so editor's first Save persists `.docx` values back to DB — next page-load sees `db_matches_extraction` and the chips no longer surface. Funding extracted as **notice-only** (not auto-applied) because `manuscript_metadata.funding_sources` is `string[]` and a single paragraph can't reliably auto-split into per-source rows. Patient-consent: 4 separate chips so editor can selectively accept the variant guess but override the institution/protocol. Top-of-form notice banner: green "✓ Pre-filled from accepted `<file_type>.docx` via Pandoc + heuristic anchor parser" on success; yellow "⚠ Could not pre-fill from .docx — extraction unavailable" with error text on failure. **PR-and-merge cadence**: commit `b0c0cf7` pushed to `claude/suspicious-banzai-dde8c9`; PR #4 opened via `gh pr create --base main`; merged via `gh pr merge 4 --merge` → `83eebe8` on `main`. Vercel auto-deployed in ~60s. `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0 (after `npm install` to hydrate the worktree's missing `node_modules` — 423 packages added; `package-lock.json` diff incidentally regenerated by npm's arch-specific binary pruning; reverted that diff via `git checkout -- package-lock.json` per explicit-path-stage protocol). Tree size 293 → 295.

#### Phase 1.5.C — Tailscale Funnel deployment (no commit; infrastructure-only)

After PR #4 landed, Kanwar smoke-tested on live `oscrsj.com` and reported "no pre-fills visible" — exactly the diagnostic surface my code was designed for, but on closer inspection the architectural cause was bigger than docx-extract. Vercel server functions calling `http://localhost:3001` (the value of `NEXT_PUBLIC_RENDERER_URL` per Session 56) have no path to Kanwar's Mac — `localhost` inside a Vercel container is the container's own loopback. This was a latent issue in BOTH Session 54's `/api/preview` proxy AND Session 57's `/api/preview/[id]` proxy; neither had been smoke-tested against a real live-prod manuscript until tonight. My docx-extract proxy + server-side direct-fetch on the panel inherited the same bug. The fix lives in deployment infrastructure, not code.

Deliberation in-session: Tailscale Funnel (free, ties to Mac uptime), Cloudflare Tunnel + new cheap domain (~$12/yr, ties to Mac uptime), Cloudflare DNS migration of oscrsj.com (free long-term, production-affecting one-hour event), Railway/Fly/Render renderer hosting (~$60/yr, Mac-independent, ~1.5-hour Dockerfile setup). Kanwar explicitly declined DNS migration ("can't we keep that system as is") and renderer-hosting ("I want to go with the free method that depends on the mac"). Tailscale Funnel chosen.

Setup steps executed via Bash + computer-use this session: (1) `brew install --cask tailscale` failed on sudo prompt (sandbox can't supply sudo password) — fell back to `brew install tailscale` (CLI-only formula, no sudo); (2) started `tailscaled --tun=userspace-networking --socket=/tmp/tailscaled.sock` in background, ran `tailscale up --hostname=oscrsj-renderer`, captured the auth URL `https://login.tailscale.com/a/e2d43be019115` for Kanwar; (5) `tailscale serve --bg http://localhost:3001` returned "Serve is not enabled on your tailnet" with admin-panel toggle URL — Kanwar enabled via that URL; (6) `tailscale serve --bg http://localhost:3001` succeeded → tailnet-only URL; (7) `tailscale funnel --bg http://localhost:3001` succeeded → **public URL `https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net`** with proxy to `http://localhost:3001`; (8) TLS handshake verified via local `curl`; (9) wrote `~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer/scripts/launchd-start.sh` (~50 lines, sets PATH explicitly because launchd doesn't inherit shell PATH, runs `git pull --ff-only`, `npm install --silent`, `npm run build`, `exec npm start`) + `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.oscrsj.renderer.plist` (KeepAlive on crash, RunAtLoad true, ThrottleInterval 30, stdout/stderr → `~/Library/Logs/oscrsj-renderer/`, `NODE_ENV=production`); validated via `plutil -lint` exit 0; macOS auto-registered the wrapper script for background activity and surfaced its "App Background Activity — launchd-start.sh can run in the background" notification; Kanwar approved via System Settings → General → Login Items & Extensions toggle. **Plist installed but not `launchctl load`-ed this session** — the existing `npm run dev` Kanwar started earlier is still bound to port 3001; loading the plist now would port-conflict with `npm start`. Plist will auto-fire on next Mac reboot via RunAtLoad. Manual `launchctl load` is documented in the wrapper script's comment block as a future option.

Kanwar updated Vercel env var `NEXT_PUBLIC_RENDERER_URL=https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net` (Production + Preview scopes, both checked) via the Vercel dashboard. Vercel auto-redeployed (~60s, "Deployment created" toast captured). Hard-reload of the manuscript admin page on live `oscrsj.com` flipped the top-of-form banner from yellow ("Could not reach renderer at http://localhost:3001/api/extract-metadata/...") to green ("✓ Pre-filled from accepted manuscript.docx via Pandoc + heuristic anchor parser"). All four §2 abstract sub-section textareas populated with HIGH-confidence chips. Architecture works end-to-end Vercel → Tailscale Funnel → Mac localhost:3001 renderer → Pandoc → extraction JSON → OSCRSJ panel → form → chips. **First time the publish workflow has ever been operational from the live `oscrsj.com` URL.**

#### Phase 1.5.D — First-publish smoke (extraction acceptance verified; full publish deferred)

Two follow-on Kanwar observations on the populated editor: (a) "only the first paragraphs from each section were taken" + (b) "the abstract wasn't filled". Both were misreads — confirmed by curling the extract endpoint directly from Mac terminal (`curl -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer $RENDERER_SHARED_SECRET" http://localhost:3001/api/extract-metadata/f8596ce8-...`) and dumping the full extracted content via Python. The Case Presentation textarea has the full 974 chars of the abstract Case Presentation (not the body — abstract is 2,211 chars total ≈ 355 words, exactly what a structured case-report abstract should be); Kanwar was seeing the textarea's 4-line scrollable viewport showing a middle portion of the content and assumed truncation. The labeled Introduction/Case Presentation/Discussion/Conclusion fields ARE the JBJS-style structured abstract, not body sections — the labels happen to match body section names. None of these need Sushant in-session; pure Kanwar-pacing.

**§6 cross-session orphan-files protocol.** OSCRSJ working tree at session start carried the Session 57 cluster of 21 untracked artifacts (Logo/, Track A Wave 1 Tracker.xlsx, pdf-design/, social-exports/, sources/, .cowork-push/, .test-uploads/, SR-MA Invited Topics docx packets, oscrsj-title-page-NEW.docx draft, push-callback-fix.sh, sample IG announcement images, wave3_leftovers_flagged.csv, etc.) — none overlapped with my 4-file edit set, all left untouched per §6 protocol. Renderer working tree at session start carried `resources/` untracked (Session 47 JATS DTD bundle) — not mine, left untouched. **No FUSE stale-lock events** this session.

**Self-improvement codification.** Two convention items worth surfacing for the next agent's Conventions §3 update:

1. **Vercel→localhost reach gap.** Any Vercel server function that fetches `http://localhost:*` is a misconfig that silently fails with "fetch failed" — `localhost` inside a Vercel serverless container is the container's own loopback, not the developer's machine. `NEXT_PUBLIC_RENDERER_URL=http://localhost:3001` (per CLAUDE.md Session 56 env state) works ONLY for client-side uses (browser fetches, `window.open` in a new tab from a Mac that's running the renderer). Server-side fetch from Vercel requires either (a) a tunnel exposing the local service publicly (Tailscale Funnel, Cloudflare Tunnel, ngrok), or (b) hosting the service on a public-reachable platform (Railway/Fly/Render/VPS). This trap caught Sessions 54 + 57 + 58 silently because no one had smoke-tested the proxy paths against a real live-prod manuscript. The pre-flight check is one curl: `curl -m 5 https://www.oscrsj.com/api/preview/<some-id>` from outside Kanwar's network — a `localhost`-misconfigured endpoint returns 502/504 instantly; a properly-tunneled one returns 200 or a real domain-specific error.

2. **Tailscale Funnel as the renderer's deployment tier-1.** `https://macbook-pro-5.tail55b3cd.ts.net` is the current canonical URL for Vercel `NEXT_PUBLIC_RENDERER_URL` env on Production + Preview. Tied to Kanwar's Mac being on + the launchd service running + the Tailscale Funnel staying authenticated. If Mac sleeps mid-publish, publish fails. Tier-2 upgrade path (when first three or four publishes have shipped + the journal has revenue): host the renderer on a Linux box (Railway/Fly/Hetzner $5-7/mo) with a Dockerfile installing Pandoc + WeasyPrint + verapdf + JATS DTD; flip the Vercel env to the public hostname; retire the Tailscale Funnel + launchd plist. The renderer code requires no changes for this migration — Tier-1 and Tier-2 are interchangeable from OSCRSJ's perspective.

**Verification.** OSCRSJ TypeScript clean exit 0 (`npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`). Renderer TypeScript clean exit 0. Pre-commit sanity per Convention §1: `git ls-tree -r HEAD --name-only | wc -l` returned 293 pre-commit + 295 post-commit (4 staged files, 2 new); `git diff --cached --stat` showed exactly 4 files / 999 insertions; explicit-path-stage no `add -A`. PR #4 merged via `gh pr merge 4 --merge` with 2 checks passing. Renderer pushed via `git push origin main` (`48d70f1..81d7985`). End-to-end smoke at the extraction layer verified by Kanwar's live-prod page load + curl verification of the full extracted content.

**Risks named.** (1) **Tailscale Funnel ties publish infra to Kanwar's Mac uptime.** Mac sleeps → publish fails; Mac restart → launchd plist auto-starts the renderer (untested until next reboot); Kanwar traveling without his Mac → can't publish; Tailscale free-tier ToS is "personal use" — commercial-journal use may be a license gray-area worth checking before scale. Tier-2 VPS migration is the documented upgrade path. (2) **launchd plist not loaded this session** — the existing dev server bound to port 3001 conflicts with the plist's `npm start`. Plist auto-fires on next reboot via RunAtLoad; if it never auto-starts cleanly (e.g., the `git pull --ff-only` errors out on a non-FF state, or the build step fails on a transient npm issue), the renderer doesn't come up + publish fails silently. Manual recovery: `tail ~/Library/Logs/oscrsj-renderer/stderr.log` to diagnose, then `launchctl unload && launchctl load ~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.oscrsj.renderer.plist` after fixing the underlying issue. (3) **Tailscale `*.ts.net` URL is hardcoded into a Vercel env var.** If the device hostname or tailnet name changes (e.g., Kanwar renames the Mac or moves to a different tailnet), the URL changes and the Vercel env needs re-updating. Low-probability; both are stable once initial-set. (4) **Renderer is still pure JATS DTD validation, JATS DTD path bug from Session 47 follow-up still unresolved** — fresh-clone-of-renderer breaks until the symlink workaround is set up. Independent of this session. (5) **First-publish smoke still gated on Kanwar's bandwidth** — running title fix + Save + Preview + render + go-live. None of these need new code; pure user-pacing. (6) **References still ship `[]` in payload** — migration 021 (structured references collection) is its own Sushant workstream; PMC indexing blocked until refs land. PDF/A-1b ships fine. (7) **DOI placeholder `10.XXXXX/oscrsj.{year}.{padded}`** until Crossref membership ($275/yr post-LLC) — Brad territory. (8) **Mac App's two Tailscale node entries** (`oscrsj-renderer` userspace from initial CLI test + `macbook-pro-5` from the Mac App) — the userspace one is now dead but still shows in Tailscale admin. Auto-prunes when Tailscale detects 90-day-offline; manual delete via admin panel takes 3 clicks if Kanwar wants it tidier sooner.

#### Phase 1.C — Preview proxy + cleanup cron + PreviewRenderCluster + renderer JATS/XMP emit (OSCRSJ commit `ed6b168`, renderer commit shipped via Kanwar Mac push)

Two-repo phase per Franklin Risk #4: OSCRSJ `/api/preview/[id]` proxies to the local renderer's `/api/preview/[id]` rather than running the chain in-Vercel — Vercel Hobby 60s timeout would clip a typical 30–45s preview render. **Renderer-side changes (5 files):** `lib/renderer/xmp.ts` — added `xmpRights_WebStatement` optional field to `XmpMetadata` interface; added to `XMP_REQUIRED_FIELDS` validator list; added `xmlns:xmpRights="http://ns.adobe.com/xap/1.0/rights/"` namespace + `<xmpRights:WebStatement>` element emit. `lib/renderer/jatsXml.ts` — `buildContribGroup` now reads `equal_contribution.present` from payload root; per-author `<xref ref-type="fn" rid="fn-equal"/>` emit when both gates hold (Janine §7.2.b); appends new `<contrib contrib-type="editor">` block sourced from `payload.handling_editor` per Janine §7.2.f. `buildCorresp` now emits a shared `<fn fn-type="equal" id="fn-equal"><p>{statement}</p></fn>` inside `<author-notes>` alongside the existing `<corresp>` block. `lib/renderer/storage.ts` — added `previewArtifactStoragePath(manuscriptId, isoTimestamp)` path builder + `writePreviewArtifact()` helper (uploads PDF to `submissions/previews/{id}/{iso}.pdf` + generates 24h signed URL + audit-log row `preview_render_artifact_uploaded` written best-effort). `lib/renderer/publishChain.ts` — added `emitPreviewArtifact?: boolean` flag to `PublishChainInput`; new branch after the `report` stage that yields `upload` + `done` events with `signedUrl` + `expiresAt` in the data; skips the regular publish-side upload + writeback entirely. New file `app/api/preview/[id]/route.ts` — mirrors `/api/publish/[id]` but calls `runPublishChain({ performPublish: false, emitPreviewArtifact: true })`; bearer-secret auth via `RENDERER_SHARED_SECRET`; UUID sanity check on `[id]`; 422 on missing payload. **Renderer commit included a bonus** — Session 56's uncommitted `lib/renderer/workspace.ts` STORAGE_BUCKET fix (manuscripts → submissions) folded in. **Kanwar pushed renderer commit from Mac terminal** mid-phase (renderer is local-only per Session 18+ pattern); confirmed back + restarted dev server. **OSCRSJ-side changes (5 files):** new `app/api/preview/[id]/route.ts` (174 lines) — admin-gated POST; synthesizes payload via `synthesizeRendererPayload` (short-circuits with 422 on synth errors — saves a wasted chain run); forwards POST to `${RENDERER_URL}/api/preview/${id}` with bearer auth + payload in body; streams renderer's NDJSON response back unchanged; audit log row `preview_render_invoked`. New `app/api/cron/cleanup-preview-artifacts/route.ts` (124 lines) — daily Vercel Cron at 15:00 UTC (after the 14:00 reminder crons and 13:00 daily-digest); walks `submissions/previews/{id}/*.pdf` + deletes objects older than 7 days; Bearer-CRON_SECRET-gated; audit log row `preview_artifacts_reaped` each run; Janine §9 retention split (7d artifacts / 90d audit-log) honored — this cron touches Storage only. New `PreviewRenderCluster.tsx` (386 lines) — Franklin §6 four-state inline-card pattern (idle / in-flight with NDJSON stage stream / success with signed URL + verdict + Regenerate / failure with first-error + expandable full-report `<details>`); stage list matches Session 47 `PublishProgress.tsx` ordering + Session 54's writeback label refinement; auto-opens signed URL in new tab on success. `MetadataEditorForm.tsx` updated: replaces Phase 1.B's alert placeholder with the cluster; §6 Preview Render section renders below §5 Validation Summary with the live preview button + scroll-flash from ValidationSummary's "Open preview ↗" anchor link. `vercel.json` registers `cleanup-preview-artifacts` cron at `0 15 * * *`. **5 files, +714/-4, TypeScript clean exit 0.** HEAD tree 290 → 293.

#### Phase 1.D — PR-and-merge + first-publish smoke deferral

Initially Phase 1.D was scoped as a 16-step end-to-end smoke against manuscript `f8596ce8`. Path-of-least-resistance changed mid-session when Kanwar elected to merge the branch to main rather than test against localhost OSCRSJ (the brief assumed a local-dev smoke). PR #1 opened via `gh pr create --base main --head claude/intelligent-swartz-5bb5f2`, merged via `gh pr merge 1 --merge` as commit `d733a64` on main, Vercel auto-deployed in ~60s with 2 checks passed. Kanwar then loaded https://www.oscrsj.com/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/f8596ce8-2424-4a47-8537-5af9e8a2f5cd in his browser and **confirmed the panel renders with all 6 sections + Validation Summary visible**. Smoke step 1–3 acceptance met. **Steps 4–16 (edit, save, preview, render, publish) deferred** — Kanwar's first-touch UX review surfaced two real issues: (a) he wants chevron-collapsible sections (Franklin originally recommended against; Kanwar overrode); (b) the abstract + COI fields aren't pre-filled because the DB stores `manuscripts.abstract` as a single TEXT blob WITHOUT the labeled anchors `Introduction:` / `Case Presentation:` etc. that the parser looks for. Kanwar's sharper architectural point: the metadata editor should be a *final-pass review against the accepted manuscript .docx*, not a *re-entry of metadata from possibly-stale DB columns* (which is what it is today because the submission wizard captures metadata at INITIAL submission, but the wizard doesn't re-capture on revision .docx upload — so after 1-2 revision rounds the DB columns are stale relative to the .docx the author actually wants published). Kanwar correctly identified this as the *whole point* of the metadata editor. **Decision (Sushant + Kanwar):** the proper fix is a new renderer endpoint `/api/extract-metadata/[id]` that runs Pandoc on the accepted .docx + extracts structured fields (abstract sections, CoI paragraph, funding, IRB statement, patient consent) for OSCRSJ to pre-fill — that's the "Body cleanup migrated from renderer cleanup pane to OSCRSJ admin" Phase 2 work the brief §10 item 7 explicitly flagged. Filed as new Sushant follow-up (~4-5 hours next session). For THIS first publish, Kanwar will manually type the abstract sections from the accepted .docx — one-time pain, ships the first manuscript, then the next Sushant session removes the pain permanently. The 16-step smoke completes after that follow-up + when Kanwar walks the manuscript through publish.

However, ONE FUSE-mount sync divergence surfaced: at Phase 1.C renderer-push boundary, Kanwar's first `git status` on Mac showed "working tree clean" while sandbox-side `git status` showed 5 staged files — Kanwar re-ran `cd ~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer && git status` and the second invocation correctly showed all 5 files. **Verification.** OSCRSJ TypeScript clean (`npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`) exit 0 on every commit boundary (Phase 1.A, 1.B, 1.C, 1.B+). **Risks named.** (1) **First-publish smoke deferred** — the 16-step verification couldn't run end-to-end this session because Kanwar's first-touch UX review surfaced the docx-extract architectural issue + he reasonably wanted that fixed before the first publish (otherwise editor has to retype abstract from .docx). Documented Sushant follow-up. (2) **Preview button won't work from prod-OSCRSJ** — Vercel functions can't reach `localhost:3001` on Kanwar's Mac; for the preview step Kanwar needs to either run OSCRSJ locally OR (Phase 2) we host the renderer somewhere reachable from Vercel. Edit, save, validate, render-published-PDF all work fine from prod. (3) **Transactional save has no rollback** — Supabase JS client has no `.transaction()`; sequential writes mean partial-fail is possible (manuscripts update succeeds, manuscript_metadata fails). Error surfaces to UI; editor retries. Phase 2 path: write a Supabase RPC stored procedure for true transactional saves. Per Brief §7 Risk #5 acknowledged. (4) **Author replace pattern (delete + bulk insert)** for `updateManuscriptMetadata` means a partial-fail where authors are deleted but insert errors out is a data-loss event. Mitigated by tx wrapping in Phase 2; for Phase 1 the risk is low (insert errors on a fresh row set are rare). Error message instructs editor to contact Sushant if it fires. (5) **JATS DTD path hotfix still pending** (CLAUDE.md open follow-up from Session 47 — `scripts/fetch-jats-dtd.sh` baked URL 404s; renderer falls back to a symlink workaround Kanwar set up). Independent of this session; surfaces again whenever a fresh renderer clone happens. (6) **References still ship `[]` in payload** — migration 021 (structured references collection) is its own Sushant workstream; PMC indexing blocked until refs land. (7) **DOI placeholder `10.XXXXX/oscrsj.{year}.{padded}`** until Crossref membership ($275/yr) is approved — Brad territory. (8) **Vercel preview proxy 60s timeout** — operating inside the budget but near the cliff per Franklin Risk #4. If a chain run exceeds 60s, the NDJSON stream cuts off and the cluster shows "Stream ended unexpectedly". Try again handles it gracefully but a single retry doesn't help if the chain is genuinely > 60s. Mitigation if hit in practice: upgrade to Vercel Pro (300s) OR host the renderer on a non-Vercel platform.

### Session 52 — 2026-05-14 — Franklin Cowork — /templates Revision Resources rewrite: removed Response Template .docx, replaced with structure-only Comment/Response/Changes instructions  ^session-52-templates-revision-resources-rewrite

**2026-05-14 Session 52 (Franklin Cowork — single-file UI fix on `/templates#revision-resources` that removes the Word-template download offering and replaces it with a structured spec authors follow in their own `.docx`).** Single commit `846ac2a` on `main`, fast-forward push (`c230d2c..846ac2a`), explicit-path stage on 1 file (`app/templates/page.tsx`), +20/-20 lines, TypeScript clean. Kanwar surfaced as a screenshot of the live `/templates#revision-resources` section plus a directive: "Remove the response template and just provide instruction on how the reviewer response document should be structure. It should address comments one by one. Users should structure the document as below: Comment: / Response: / Changes: / Changes should reference line numbers."

**The change.** Two new white cards inside the same `<section id="revision-resources">` (anchor preserved so transactional decision-email links from `editorialDecisionMinorRevisions.ts` + `editorialDecisionMajorRevisions.ts` still navigate correctly post-push):

- **How to structure your Response to Reviewers document** — instructs authors to prepare a single Word doc (.docx) grouped by reviewer (Reviewer 1, Reviewer 2, …), addressing every comment one by one in the order reviewers raised them. A code-style `<pre>` block shows the three-label structure (`Comment:` / `Response:` / `Changes:`) followed by a `<ul>` defining each label. `Comment:` quotes the reviewer comment verbatim. `Response:` is the author's reply — agreement, disagreement, clarification, or explanation. `Changes:` describes the specific change made to the manuscript and explicitly cites line numbers in the revised manuscript. If the author declines to make a change, "No change" goes under `Changes:` and the rationale goes under `Response:`.
- **How to format your tracked-changes manuscript** — preserved from before (RED font + YELLOW highlight on every changed/added word, separate file from clean revised manuscript), just moved into its own card with its own H3 heading.

**What was removed.** The right-rail download button (`<a href="/downloads/oscrsj-revision-response-template.docx" download className="btn-primary-light">Download Response Template (.docx)</a>`) and the prose paragraph "A Word template for assembling your point-by-point response to reviewer comments. Includes a fully worked example (3 sample comments with author responses, change descriptions, and line-number references) plus pre-formatted blank response tables you populate." The two-column flex layout (text on left + sidebar button on right) collapsed into a single-column card stack.

**Styling decisions.** Code-style `<pre>` block uses `bg-cream-alt border border-border rounded-lg p-4 text-sm text-ink font-mono leading-relaxed mb-4 whitespace-pre-wrap` — matches the design system's "subtle highlight surface" rule (cream-alt for chips/pills/code) and the existing `font-mono` usage elsewhere on the site (`app/page.tsx`, `app/for-reviewers/page.tsx`, admin manuscript pages). Bullet list under the code block uses `list-disc ml-5 space-y-2` for readable density. Em-dashes properly HTML-encoded as `&mdash;`, single quotes as `&rsquo;`, double quotes as `&ldquo;` / `&rdquo;` for typography-clean source — matches the existing typographic convention in `app/templates/page.tsx`.

**Scope decision — Adjacent-Discrepancy Protocol invoked.** A pre-flight grep of `Revision Response Template|revision-response-template|Response Template` across the repo surfaced **4 downstream surfaces** that name the (now-removed) template by reference:

- `lib/email/templates/editorialDecisionMinorRevisions.ts` line 81 (HTML body) + line 119 (plain-text body) — "Use the OSCRSJ Revision Response Template (worked example + pre-formatted response tables): https://www.oscrsj.com/templates#revision-resources"
- `lib/email/templates/editorialDecisionMajorRevisions.ts` lines 82 + 120 — same lines
- `app/dashboard/submit/Step2Files.tsx:193` Response-to-Reviewers file description — "Use the OSCRSJ Revision Response Template (download from /templates) — it contains a worked example and pre-formatted response tables. Accepted formats: .docx or .pdf (max 20 MB)."
- `lib/reviewer-feedback/build.ts:228` author-facing italic note inside the blinded reviewer-feedback `.docx` itself — "Please address each comment point-by-point in your Response to Reviewers letter using the Revision Response Template available at oscrsj.com/templates."

Plus 2 orphaned-but-unreferenced assets: `scripts/build-revision-response-template.py` (build script) and `public/downloads/oscrsj-revision-response-template.docx` (the `.docx` file itself, still served if the URL is memorized).

Per Session 50's Adjacent-Discrepancy Protocol — "fix only what the user explicitly named + flag the rest as new follow-up items; never silently expand scope to 'while we're at it' sweeps" — this commit stayed scoped to the templates page Kanwar named, and the 4 stale surfaces are flagged in CLAUDE.md §11 Open Follow-ups for a coherent next-session sweep. Trade-off: until that sweep lands, decision emails + Step 2 wizard + reviewer-feedback `.docx` still tell users to use a template that doesn't exist on the page anymore. Severity assessed as low because the `#revision-resources` anchor still resolves cleanly post-commit — users following the email link land on the new structure cards and infer the right behavior even though the email language is slightly stale.

**§6 cross-session orphan-files protocol noise.** Working tree at session start carried 21 untracked artifacts (lockfile remnants, downloaded sample docs, social-export drafts, draft tracking spreadsheets, `.cowork-push/` recovery patch from Sessions 49/50, `.test-uploads/`, `Logo/`, `pdf-design/`, `sources/`, `wave3_leftovers_flagged.csv`, etc.). None overlapped with the templates-page edit; all left untouched per §6 ("If `git status` shows pre-staged files you did not touch in the current session, STOP"). The recovery patch from Sessions 49/50 (`.cowork-push/session-49-50-orphan-decision-composer-feature.patch`) is now historical — Session 51 resolved it.

**FUSE stale-lock event.** Hit `.git/index.lock` + `.git/HEAD.lock` simultaneously at first `git add`; cleared via the documented `mv .git/X.lock .git/.stale-junk/X.lock.$(date +%s%N)` workaround. Standard `tmp_obj_*` "Operation not permitted" unlink warnings during `git commit` — non-fatal per Conventions "FUSE stale-lock workaround" — commit + push completed cleanly.

**TypeScript clean.** `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0 pre-commit. HEAD tree size 269 (healthy; Session 51 finished at 268, this commit adds 0 new files).

**Risks.** (1) The 4 downstream stale references create a temporary "the email tells me to use a template I can't find" gap; severity low because the page anchor still navigates to coherent instructions and authors can copy the three-label structure directly into Word. (2) Visual spot-check at three breakpoints not run from sandbox — the `<pre>` block + bullet list layout at narrow mobile widths needs a Kanwar eyeball after Vercel deploys. (3) The `oscrsj-revision-response-template.docx` file in `public/downloads/` is now orphan-served — still accessible if someone has the URL memorized; cleanup deferred to the downstream-surfaces sweep.

**Self-improvement note worth folding.** Suggested Conventions §3 addition (vault + CLAUDE.md): "**Removed-artifact downstream-surface inventory.** When removing a user-facing artifact (downloadable template, link target, brand asset, file from `public/`), grep for every surface that names it by reference *before* scoping the commit. Either bundle the rewrite into one coherent commit with permission, or flag every stale surface explicitly in the wrap-up so the user can decide. Silent half-fixes that leave emails or form descriptions pointing at deleted assets are worse than a slightly-broader commit." Codifying this would have made the scope-decision step here mechanical instead of judgment-call.

**Kanwar follow-ups.** (1) Visual spot-check `/templates#revision-resources` post-Vercel-deploy (~60s after `846ac2a`) at three breakpoints — confirm the code-style `<pre>` block renders cleanly on mobile + the bullet list is readable + the tracked-changes card flows correctly below; (2) decide whether the 4 downstream stale references get fixed as a single coherent follow-up commit or stay deferred indefinitely (the `.docx` file orphan is harmless, the email references are mildly confusing); (3) consider whether `scripts/build-revision-response-template.py` and `public/downloads/oscrsj-revision-response-template.docx` should be deleted from the repo entirely once the downstream sweep lands.

**Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 51 — 2026-05-14 — Sushant Cowork — reviewer-feedback Word doc attaches to Minor/Major Revisions decision emails (orphan-cluster recovery + lib/admin/actions.ts wiring)  ^session-51-reviewer-feedback-attachment

**2026-05-14 Session 51 (Sushant Cowork — closes the long-running "send the author a Word doc of the reviewer feedback alongside the decision letter" feature in a single working session by recovering the orphaned Session 49 cluster + rebuilding the actions.ts wiring that was wiped during Session 50's TYPE_LABELS revert).** Single feature commit `3c3d8f5` on `main`, fast-forward push (`de00f6c..3c3d8f5`), explicit-path stage on 5 files (4 modified + 1 new), 954 insertions / 11 deletions, TypeScript clean. Kanwar surfaced the feature as a question — "I remember saying I want a system in which a word document is sent to the author when sending them a decision for minor or major revision. Where are we with that?" — and the answer was "~80% built, sitting orphaned in a recovery patch since Session 49, nothing committed yet." This session closes that gap.

**The feature, behaviorally.** When an editor sends a Minor Revisions or Major Revisions decision on a manuscript that has at least one submitted review carrying author-facing comments, the decision email now ships with a blinded `{submissionId}-reviewer-feedback.docx` attached. The doc contains every reviewer's `comments_to_author` text, labelled `Reviewer 1`, `Reviewer 2`, etc. in `submitted_date` ASC order so reviewer labels stay stable across re-invite rounds. Reviewer identities, scores, recommendations, and `comments_to_editor` are NEVER included. The decision-email body copy adapts: the HTML + plain-text bodies surface a paragraph explicitly telling the author the .docx is attached and confidential, and the "Response to Reviewers" instructions flip from "shared with you" to "in the attached feedback document." The editor has a Preview button in the decision composer to review the auto-generated .docx in their browser before sending; if they want to edit it (rewrite passages, redact, reorder, add editorial notes), they download, edit in Word, and upload the edited version as an override — `submitEditorialDecision` ships the override instead of regenerating fresh.

**What committed where.**

- **`lib/reviewer-feedback/build.ts`** (new, 382 lines) — pizzip-only .docx generator. Hand-assembles `[Content_Types].xml` + `_rels/.rels` + `word/_rels/document.xml.rels` + `word/styles.xml` (Title style: 24pt bold #3D2A18 brown / Heading 1 style: 16pt bold #3D2A18 brown / Calibri 11pt body) + `word/document.xml` (title paragraph + metadata block carrying `Submission ID: ${submissionId}` bold + title italic brown + `Issued: ${dateLabel}` + author-facing note in italic warm gray + per-reviewer Heading 1 + body paragraphs preserving blank-line + soft-break formatting via the `commentsToParagraphs` splitter — splits on `\n\s*\n+` for paragraph boundaries, single `\n` becomes a `<w:br/>` soft break). Output filename slugified from `submission_id` (already ASCII-safe in practice but guards against future format changes). Reviewers ordered by `submitted_date` ASC with `created_at` ASC tiebreaker. Reviews where `comments_to_author` is null or whitespace are silently skipped; if every review is empty, returns `{ empty: true }` and caller ships the decision email without attachment (templates render cleanly via the `hasReviewerFeedbackAttachment: false` default copy path). PizZip generates with `DEFLATE` compression level 6 returning a `nodebuffer`. No external docx library — pizzip-only keeps the dependency footprint identical to the existing reviewer-package build pipeline (Session 31).
- **`lib/admin/actions.ts`** (+255/-1) — adds:
  - Import for `buildReviewerFeedbackDocx` and `SendEmailAttachment` type
  - `SubmitEditorialDecisionArgs.reviewerFeedbackOverride?: { filename: string; contentBase64: string } | null` field (editor-edited override; `null` or omitted means use auto-generated)
  - `REVIEWER_FEEDBACK_MAX_BYTES = 22 * 1024 * 1024` constant (Resend caps total payload at ~40 MB; base64 inflates ~33%, so 22 MB raw is the safe ceiling for a single attachment with email body + shell rendered)
  - `ResolvedReviewerFeedbackAttachment` exported interface (attachment + hasAttachment + source: 'override'|'auto'|'none' + reviewerCount + error)
  - `resolveReviewerFeedbackAttachment(manuscriptId, override?)` exported async function — single source of truth for the override-vs-auto resolution. Override path decodes base64, enforces 22 MB cap, returns `SendEmailAttachment` ready for Resend. Auto path calls `buildReviewerFeedbackDocx`, also enforces 22 MB cap. On any hard failure (decode error, oversize, DB error), returns `{ attachment: null, hasAttachment: false, error: '...' }` and the caller ships the email without attachment + with default no-attachment copy. Exposed as a top-level export so future paths (resend, regenerate-on-demand, bulk decision flows) can share the logic.
  - `PreviewReviewerFeedbackArgs` + `PreviewReviewerFeedbackResult` interfaces
  - `previewReviewerFeedback({ manuscriptId })` exported server action — gated on `requireEditorOrAdmin`. Builds the .docx, returns `{ ok: true, empty: false, filename, contentBase64, reviewerCount }` for non-empty builds; `{ ok: true, empty: true, ... }` when no reviewer comments exist yet (UI surfaces "No completed reviewer comments are available yet for this manuscript. No attachment will be sent." amber notice).
  - In `submitEditorialDecision`, the `minor_revisions` and `major_revisions` branches each now call `resolveReviewerFeedbackAttachment` before constructing the email, pass `hasReviewerFeedbackAttachment: resolved.hasAttachment` into the renderer, and conditionally spread `attachments: [resolved.attachment]` into the `sendEmail` call (omitted entirely when no attachment to ship — keeps the call shape identical to legacy decisions). Each branch then writes a `reviewer_feedback_attachment_resolved` row to `audit_logs` capturing decision type, source (override/auto/none), `has_attachment`, `reviewer_count`, and any error — gives Manvir an audit trail for "did this decision ship with reviewer feedback attached, and where did the attachment come from?". Audit-log failure is swallowed (logging must never break the send).
- **`app/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/[id]/DecisionComposerPanel.tsx`** (+268, from the recovered orphan patch) — new "Reviewer feedback attachment" card visible only when `decision === 'minor_revisions' || decision === 'major_revisions'` and `!deskRejectMode`. The card holds explanatory copy (what gets attached, that identities aren't included, how to override) + three buttons:
  - **"Preview / download attachment"** → calls `previewReviewerFeedback({ manuscriptId })`, decodes base64 via `atob` → `Uint8Array` → `Blob` with MIME `application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document`, creates an object URL, programmatically clicks an `<a download>` element, revokes the URL after a 1500ms grace period. Surfaces a green message with reviewer count on success or amber "no reviewer comments yet" notice on `empty: true`.
  - **"Upload edited version"** → hidden file input restricted to `.docx`. On change: 22 MB client-side cap (matches server-side `REVIEWER_FEEDBACK_MAX_BYTES`), MIME + extension check, `FileReader.readAsDataURL` → slice the base64 payload after the `,` separator, stash `{ filename, contentBase64, sizeBytes }` in state. Surfaces a green "Override active: filename · KB" banner.
  - **"Clear override"** → resets the override state, surfaces "Override cleared. The auto-generated reviewer feedback document will be attached when you submit."
  On `onSubmit`, the override (if any) flows through to `submitEditorialDecision` as `reviewerFeedbackOverride: { filename, contentBase64 }`. Override state is cleared in the success branch alongside the rest of the form reset. The card never renders for `accept`, `post_review_reject`, `reject`, or `desk_reject` decisions — no reviewer feedback to attach there.
- **`lib/email/templates/editorialDecisionMinorRevisions.ts`** and **`editorialDecisionMajorRevisions.ts`** (each +30/-5, from the recovered orphan patch) — both now accept `hasReviewerFeedbackAttachment?: boolean` on their params interface. When `true`, both the HTML body (via the `paragraph(...)` helper) and the plain-text body get a leading attachment paragraph: "The full reviewer comments are attached to this email as **{submissionId}-reviewer-feedback.docx**. Please consult that document alongside the editor's letter below as you prepare your revision. Reviewer identities are kept confidential." The "Response to Reviewers (.docx)" instruction in the numbered list also adapts via inline template literal: when `hasReviewerFeedbackAttachment`, the copy reads "For every reviewer comment **in the attached feedback document**, quote..."; when false, the legacy "shared with you" phrasing renders. Session 50 reverted 3 of those (DecisionComposerPanel + both email templates) to HEAD via `git show + mv` workaround so its TYPE_LABELS-on-actions.ts edit could ship clean; captured the 3-file diff as a 492-line unified-diff patch at `.cowork-push/session-49-50-orphan-decision-composer-feature.patch`; `lib/admin/actions.ts` orphan additions (import, interface field, 2 in-function `feedbackAttachment` blocks, 2 new exports) were ALSO wiped during that revert and were NOT captured in the patch (intentional — they could be reconstructed from the email-template prop shape + DecisionComposerPanel call shape); `lib/reviewer-feedback/build.ts` was left untracked on disk as a recovery package. This session: (a) ran `git apply --check .cowork-push/session-49-50-orphan-decision-composer-feature.patch` → clean; (b) applied the patch → 3 files restored to their Session-49 working-tree state; (c) read the current `submitEditorialDecision` flow (lines 705-738) + the SendEmailAttachment shape (lib/email/resend.ts lines 45-53) + the existing `requireEditorOrAdmin` gate pattern + the existing audit-log insert pattern; (d) rebuilt `lib/admin/actions.ts` wiring from scratch per the spec captured in Session 50's wrap-up narrative (CLAUDE.md §11 Sushant handoff bullet). The rebuild is functionally equivalent to the orphan additions but is fresh code, not recovered code — no claim that this matches the original implementation byte-for-byte, just that it satisfies the same contract.

**Risks (acknowledged + addressed).**

1. **22 MB cap can be hit in pathological cases.** A manuscript with 5+ reviewers, each writing 50+ pages of comments with embedded soft breaks, could in theory exceed 22 MB. Reality check: blinded text-only .docx with no images compresses heavily under DEFLATE; the largest realistic reviewer-feedback document is probably <500 KB. Both override + auto paths enforce the cap server-side; on bust, attachment is dropped + email ships with default copy + audit log captures the error. No data loss — the reviews remain in the DB, the author can be sent the feedback manually.
2. **Stale TYPE_LABELS reference from Session 50 in MANUSCRIPT_TYPE_LABELS.** Not touched this session — the type-label dict for `'review_article' → 'Systematic Review & Meta-Analysis'` from Session 50 remains correct. No regression.
3. **Audit log writes use `(admin.from(...) as any).insert(...)` cast.** Matches the existing pattern at `lib/admin/actions.ts:1248` for the prior reviewer-re-invite audit log — `audit_logs.details` is `jsonb` which the generated DB types don't always reflect. Cast is intentional + matches established convention. Not a separate convention to codify.
4. **Override .docx not parsed before send.** The editor could upload a corrupted/non-.docx file (or a file someone tampered with locally). Server-side validation = filename + size + MIME-via-extension only; we don't crack the .docx open to verify it's well-formed. Acceptable: the editor is a trusted role with `requireEditorOrAdmin` gating, and Resend will reject the attachment if it's malformed enough to fail send → caller gets `sendError` from the existing `sendEmail` try/catch. No silent data corruption.
5. **`/templates#revision-resources` anchor existence verified pre-flip** via grep at `app/templates/page.tsx:326` (`<section id="revision-resources">`). If the anchor ever moves, both email templates need updating in lockstep. Worth a future Convention §3 entry: "Outbound email anchors must be grep-verified pre-flip."
6. **No smoke test run in-session.** Smoke requires a manuscript with `status = 'under_review'` + `>=1` submitted non-draft review carrying `comments_to_author` text + an editor user — none reliably present in dev/prod at session time. Kanwar follow-up below.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` from repo root → exit 0. HEAD tree size 267 → 268 (one new file: `lib/reviewer-feedback/build.ts`). Working-tree diff staged exactly the 5 expected files (4 modified + 1 new), 954 insertions, 11 deletions — well within the "100+ files = red flag" range per CLAUDE.md Git Safety §2 sanity-check. Explicit-path stage; no `git add -A`. Fast-forward push to `origin/main` confirmed via `git log -1 origin/main --oneline` = `3c3d8f5`.

**FUSE artifact noise.** Standard stale tmp_obj_* + `.git/HEAD.lock` unlink-not-permitted warnings during `git add` + `git commit` — all non-fatal per CLAUDE.md Conventions "FUSE stale-lock workaround." Operations completed cleanly; no `mv` workaround required this session (warnings only, no blocking errors).

Click "Preview / download attachment" → verify the .docx downloads + opens in Word with title + Reviewer 1 / Reviewer 2 sections rendering correctly. Submit Minor Revisions decision → verify the corresponding-author receives the email with attachment + the body copy references the .docx + the `audit_logs` table has a `reviewer_feedback_attachment_resolved` row with `source: 'auto'`. ### Session 47 — 2026-05-06 — Sushant Cowork — JATS Publishing 1.3 XML capability landed in renderer + main-repo migration 020 + admin panel + first GitHub-ready push of renderer  ^session-47-jats-xml-implementation

**2026-05-06 Session 47 (Sushant Cowork — JATS Publishing 1.3 XML capability landed in renderer + main-repo migration 020 + admin panel + first GitHub-ready push of renderer).** Closes Manvir handoff `^handoff-jats-xml-implementation-2026-05-05` (P0 — gates Gate 5 PMC application path; every article published without JATS becomes retroactive PMC technical debt). Two repos touched: `~/Documents/oscrsj-renderer/` ships two commits (`47229a2` orphan landing + `d28e27c` JATS feature) and `~/Documents/OSCRSJ/OSCRSJ/` ships one commit on `main` (migration 020 + types + admin panel + admin/actions.ts).

**Architecture decision (Session 47).** JATS XML is constructed directly in TypeScript via small element-builder helpers in `lib/renderer/jatsXml.ts` rather than running a second Python/Jinja2 subprocess. The HTML render path uses Jinja2 because `template.html` has heavy formatting logic (dropcaps, @page rules, figure layout). JATS is structurally mechanical and the payload is already JSON — a TypeScript element-builder is more testable, deploys with no extra Python dependency, and gives us deterministic output for references and figures. Resolves spec §13 Q5 — Pandoc Vancouver-with-DOI flattening risk avoided by emitting `<ref-list>` ourselves from the structured `references[]` array. Pandoc IS still used, but only for body content (cleanedHtml → JATS body fragment via `pandoc --from html --to jats`); we strip any embedded `<ref-list>` Pandoc produces and replace with the canonical version.

**5 day-1 spec questions (§13) all resolved.** Q1 — Pandoc 2.9.2 sandbox emits a body fragment without DOCTYPE; we wrap with JATS 1.3 DOCTYPE in the article shell ourselves. Production runs against Pandoc 3.1+ (the renderer's existing probe in `lib/pandoc.ts` enforces this — no change needed). Q2 — Volume/issue fall back to `payload.issue.volume` / `issue_number`, present in every fixture; Manvir locks at acceptance. Q3 — Publisher legal entity hardcoded to "OSCRSJ — Publisher TBD pending LLC" until Brad's LLC lands. Q4 — CRediT roles emit free-text in `<role>`, no controlled-vocabulary mapping. Q5 — references generated directly from structured payload, `<mixed-citation publication-type="journal">` with `<pub-id pub-id-type="doi">` when DOI present.

**License URL locked to CC BY 4.0** (canonical `https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/`) per Janine handoff `^handoff-license-revert-code-surfaces-2026-05-04` (2026-05-04). The `ali:license_ref` attribute and the `<license-p>` body text are hardcoded in `buildPermissions()` regardless of what the payload's `article.license` object says — payload-level license strings are LEGACY (CC BY-NC-ND in the v1.0 fixtures); journal policy reverted to CC BY 4.0 on 2026-05-04 and JATS reflects that lock. This is integration point #3 from Janine's 4-point license-revert handoff (the JATS XML license URL deliverable).
