### Session 45 — 2026-05-05 — Sushant Cowork — daily editorial-ops digest cron  ^session-45-daily-editorial-ops-digest-cron

**2026-05-05 Session 45 (Sushant Cowork — daily editorial-ops digest cron). Single commit on `main` (3 files changed: 2 new, 1 modified, plus this CLAUDE.md changelog).** Triggered by Kanwar same-day directive: "I would like to be sent an email once someone registers to make an account on our journal website with their information. I would also like a notification when someone submits a manuscript ... I also dont want to overflow my email when this picks up." Architecture decision (made by Manvir before delegating to Sushant): **single daily digest, NOT per-event emails** — per-event emails compound badly at scale (5-10/day by month 6 mixed with reviewer correspondence + bounce notifications), and migrating from per-event → digest later is annoying because it trains the wrong inbox muscle memory. Digest pattern reuses the existing cron + email_logs + audit_logs infrastructure: **(a)** new `app/api/cron/daily-digest/route.ts` (~330 lines, pattern-matched against `/api/cron/review-reminders/route.ts` and `/api/cron/revision-reminders/route.ts`), bearer-header gated by `CRON_SECRET` (same secret as the existing two crons), `runtime: 'nodejs'`, `dynamic: 'force-dynamic'`. Five parallel queries via `Promise.all` over a rolling 24h window: `users.created_at`, `manuscripts WHERE submission_date IN window AND status != 'draft'`, `manuscript_revisions.submitted_date`, `reviewer_applications.created_at`, `reviews WHERE is_draft = false AND submitted_date IN window`. Two enrichment queries follow: a manuscripts batch fetch keyed by `id` for revisions + reviews that need title + submission_id (reuses already-loaded rows from the submissions section to avoid double-fetch), and a users batch fetch for corresponding-author display names + reviewer names. Reviewer-name resolution handles BOTH paths — registered reviewers via `reviewer_id` user lookup, and external invitees via `review_invitation_id_snapshot_email` → `review_invitations.reviewer_first_name + reviewer_last_name` snapshot lookup, falling back to the bare email when names aren't on the invitation row. **Empty-window short-circuit:** if all five counts are zero, the cron writes a single `daily_digest_skipped_empty` audit_log row and returns `{sent: false, reason: 'no_activity'}` — never sends a zero-noise email. Non-empty windows fire ONE Resend email via the existing `sendEmail()` wrapper with `emailType: 'daily_digest'` (so it lands in `email_logs` for delivery tracking like every other transactional send) and audit-log `daily_digest_sent` carrying counts + recipient + message_id. **(b)** new `lib/email/templates/dailyDigest.ts` (~340 lines) using the v2 dark-brown shell from Session 30 (`renderEmailShell` + `paragraph` + `escapeHtml` + `plainTextFooter`). Five sections render conditionally (skipped when count is 0): New registrations · New manuscript submissions · Revisions received · New reviewer applications · Reviews submitted. Each section renders a count-suffixed sub-heading + per-item card on the faint warm-gray detailsBg (#F7F6F4) with brown-dark left accent border, plus a "Open in admin →" deep link beneath each card pointing at the relevant `/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/[id]` or `/dashboard/admin/reviewer-applications` page. Plain-text body mirrors the HTML in flat list form for clients that strip HTML. Subject line carries machine-readable counts: `[OSCRSJ Daily] 3 registrations · 1 submission · 2 revisions` so Kanwar can read the digest from the inbox preview without opening it. Recipient resolution ladder via new `digestRecipient()` helper: `DIGEST_RECIPIENT_EMAIL` env var → `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` env var → hardcoded `kanwarparhar@gmail.com` fallback (matches the established pattern from `lib/reviewer/actions.ts:61`). Default behavior: digest goes to whatever Reply-To is currently set to (effectively the editorial inbox). **(c)** `vercel.json` gains a third cron entry at `0 13 * * *` — staggered one hour earlier than the existing review-reminders + revision-reminders crons (both at `0 14 * * *`) so the three runs spread across the cron quota window. Subject-line wording and section ordering chosen so a Gmail filter can label the digest "OSCRSJ ops" without false positives — every send carries the literal `[OSCRSJ Daily]` subject prefix and originates from the same Resend `EMAIL_FROM` address. **No new migration. No new env vars required to deploy** — `DIGEST_RECIPIENT_EMAIL` is optional (the existing `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` env var on Vercel is already set to `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` per Session 43 flip-in-flight, which is the desired digest target). `CRON_SECRET` is already provisioned per Session 11. **No new routes** in the user-facing sense — the cron endpoint is admin-internal. **§6 separation-of-concerns event** at session-start: working tree carried the same 5 unstaged orphan files Session 44 flagged (`.env.example`, `app/api/submissions/[id]/co-author-dispute/route.ts`, `lib/email/resend.ts`, `lib/email/templates/reviewerApplicationInternalNotification.ts`, `lib/submission/actions.ts`) from the still-in-flight editorial-inbox-routing flip session. Per §6, all 5 left unstaged for that owner-session to resume. PLUS 2 intervening commits since Session 44 wrap (`8dbea9d` Yash Mehta credentials full update, `4c8eefa` Yash Mehta credentials simplify) still not backfilled in CLAUDE.md — same backfill-debt pattern Session 44 itself flagged for `4aae9f1`/`2290bae`/`5808bae`. Not my work, not addressed this session per §6 (reconstructing from `git log` alone risks mis-description); flagged for the next CEO-led wrap session. Tree size 262 → 264 (+2: cron route + email template). `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exit 0. Fast-forward push from `4c8eefa`, explicit-path stage only (`app/api/cron/daily-digest/route.ts`, `lib/email/templates/dailyDigest.ts`, `vercel.json`, `CLAUDE.md`), no `add -A`, no force. **Risks worth naming:** (1) **Vercel Hobby cron quota.** Hobby tier historically allowed up to 2 cron jobs running once-per-day; this session adds a third. If Vercel rejects the deploy with a quota error, the fix options are (a) upgrade to Pro ($20/mo, also unlocks 60s function timeout helpful for the Session 31 reviewer-package build), (b) consolidate one of the existing crons into the digest's run path, (c) downgrade the digest to a manual-trigger-only endpoint until upgrade. The cron entry compiles and the route works fine on its own — the risk is Vercel platform-side, not code-side. (2) **Default recipient assumption.** Without `DIGEST_RECIPIENT_EMAIL` set, the digest defaults to `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` which Session 43 set to `oscrsjournal@gmail.com`. If Kanwar wants the digest to go to a different inbox than the editorial Reply-To target (e.g., his personal Gmail with a separate filter), set `DIGEST_RECIPIENT_EMAIL` on Vercel; otherwise the default behavior is correct. (3) **24h rolling window vs. calendar day.** The cron tick at 13:00 UTC daily means the window is "prior 24h from now" — yesterday 13:00 UTC → today 13:00 UTC. DST transitions cause a 1h skew once a year (negligible for a digest's purpose). If Kanwar wants strict calendar-day alignment, that's a 5-line edit to the cron route to round to the previous midnight in his preferred timezone. (4) **Reviewer-name resolution for direct-email invitees who haven't been snapshotted.** If the `review_invitations` row's `reviewer_first_name`/`reviewer_last_name` columns are NULL (early Session 9 invitations pre-dating the snapshot columns), the digest falls back to displaying the bare email. Acceptable; the email is a unique identifier. (5) **First-day digest will likely send empty.** At pre-launch volume, most days have 0 events. The empty-window short-circuit handles this — Kanwar won't see a zero-noise email — but he should not interpret silence as a delivery failure. The audit_log `daily_digest_skipped_empty` row is the proof-of-life record. **Kanwar follow-up actions (in priority order):** (1) **Verify deploy succeeds.** Vercel auto-deploys ~60s post-push. If the deploy fails citing cron quota, the build log will say so explicitly — escalate to me for the consolidation pivot. (2) **Smoke test by manual trigger.** Once deployed, run `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET" https://www.oscrsj.com/api/cron/daily-digest` from your terminal (replace `$CRON_SECRET` with the value from your Vercel env vars). Expected response: `{"sent": false, "reason": "no_activity", ...}` if no events have happened in the last 24h, OR `{"sent": true, "recipient": "...", "subject": "[OSCRSJ Daily] ...", ...}` if there's been activity. Either way the response should be 200, NOT 401 (401 means CRON_SECRET mismatch) and NOT 500 (500 means the route hit an unhandled error). (3) **Force a non-empty test by registering a fake user.** Open `/register` in a private window, sign up with a throwaway email (e.g., your Gmail with a `+test45` alias), then re-run the manual trigger curl. The response should now show `"sent": true` and `counts.registrationCount: 1`, and your inbox at `oscrsjournal@gmail.com` (or whatever `EMAIL_REPLY_TO` resolves to) should receive the digest within ~5s. The subject line should read `[OSCRSJ Daily] 1 registration`. (4) **Set up the Gmail filter.** Once the first real digest lands, create a filter: matches `subject:"[OSCRSJ Daily]"`, applies label `OSCRSJ ops`, marks as read OR keeps in inbox depending on preference. The bracket prefix is the stable matchable token. (5) **Inspect `email_logs` + `audit_logs`** after the smoke test — should see one new `email_logs` row with `email_type = 'daily_digest'` and one `audit_logs` row with `action = 'daily_digest_sent'` (or `daily_digest_skipped_empty` for empty windows). (6) **Optional `DIGEST_RECIPIENT_EMAIL` env var** — set on Vercel only if you want the digest to land somewhere other than the existing Reply-To target. (7) **Kanwar end-of-Phase-3 smoke test** (carried over from Session 44 backlog) — separate, not blocked by this session. **Handoffs pushed: None** (the digest is a cross-cutting feature; downstream agents don't need notification — the next session's owner-agent will see it in CLAUDE.md). **Self-improvement notes for next session's owner-agent:** the digest deliberately skips two event types Manvir considered + dropped: **manuscript withdrawals** (require audit_logs scanning since `manuscripts.status` lacks a `status_changed_at` column — could be added in a future schema-snapshot pass that also unblocks the Session 14 revision-diff feature), and **editorial decisions issued** (the editor doing the issuing is the digest recipient, so surfacing them back to him is redundant — unless multiple editors come online, in which case re-evaluate). Both are easy adds when the time comes; the section-renderer pattern in `dailyDigest.ts` is meant to be extended.

### Session 44 — 2026-05-04 — Sushant Cowork — admin manuscript "Reviewer pool" panel sources from unified roster, not legacy literal status  ^session-44-admin-manuscript-reviewer-pool

**2026-05-04 Session 44 (Sushant Cowork — admin manuscript "Reviewer pool" panel sources from unified roster, not legacy literal status). Single code commit `5bea320` on `main` (`b1b548a..5bea320`, fast-forward push, 3 files, +221/-62, `tsc --noEmit` clean, explicit-path stage only).** Triggered by Kanwar reporting that a freshly-submitted manuscript (`OSCRSJ-2026-0032`, bilateral acute exertional compartment syndrome case report, status=`submitted`) showed an empty "Active reviewer pool" on the admin detail page, while the Reviewers tab on the left sidebar (`/dashboard/admin/reviewer-applications?tab=active`) clearly listed one reviewer (Manvir Kaur) in Active status. **Diagnosis via live SQL against Supabase prod** (Chrome MCP → SQL Editor at `uambcccuookmbciqpcvh.supabase.co`): two surfaces using two different definitions of "active" by construction. Population A — `reviewer_applications WHERE status = 'active'` (the literal column filter the admin manuscript panel was reading via `listReviewerApplications({ status: 'active' })`): **0 rows in the entire DB.** Population B — the Session 32 unified roster's "active" bucket, computed dynamically as `reviewsSubmitted > 0` (`buildReviewerRoster()` line 2240): **1 row, Manvir Kaur (manvirgill96@gmail.com), `applicationId: null`, 1 review submitted on a prior manuscript.** Manvir was invited via the Session 11.5 direct-email path which bypasses `reviewer_applications` entirely, so the literal column filter cannot see her by construction; even if she'd had an application row, the Session 32 unified roster never WRITES `status = 'active'` to that column (the bucket is purely computed), so no organic post-Session-32 "active" reviewer can ever satisfy the literal filter. **Fix (3 files, single commit `5bea320`):** (a) `app/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/[id]/page.tsx` — replaced the `listReviewerApplications({ status: 'active' })` call with `buildReviewerRoster()` parallel-fetch + side-loaded `listReviewerApplications({ status: 'all' })` to preserve subspecialty-interest ranking (the roster entry shape doesn't carry `subspecialty_interests`). Filters the roster to bucket NOT IN (`declined`, `withdrawn`) AND legacy `applicationStatus` NOT IN (`declined`, `withdrawn`) for defense-in-depth against pre-Session-32 editor-triaged decline rows that would otherwise re-surface. Builds a flat `ReviewerPoolEntry[]` carrying email + name + applicationId | null + bucket + subspecialtyInterests + careerStage + reviewsSubmitted + latestInvitationDate. (b) `app/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/[id]/InviteReviewerPanel.tsx` — full rewrite of the panel's data contract. New `ReviewerPoolEntry` exported interface; `alreadyInvited` dedupe flipped from `applicationId` (covered the application path only) to lowercased `email` (covers BOTH paths — application-mode invites carry `reviewer_application_id` AND `reviewer_email` snapshot; direct-email invites carry only `reviewer_email`). Per-row dispatch: `inviteReviewer({ mode: 'application', reviewerApplicationId })` when `applicationId` is set; `inviteReviewer({ mode: 'email', reviewerEmail, reviewerFirstName, reviewerLastName })` otherwise. New per-row bucket pill (active green / approved green / pending amber / applicant blue / declined red / withdrawn neutral) so editors see status at a glance. New "N reviews submitted" badge on rows with non-zero `reviewsSubmitted` for proven-reviewer signal. Sort key: subspecialty match > bucket priority (active > approved > pending > applicant) > last-name alphabetical. Header renamed `Active reviewer pool` → `Reviewer pool` since "active" is now overloaded between the legacy column and the computed bucket. Subhead added: "Sourced from the unified reviewer roster — proven reviewers, accepted invitees, applicants from /for-reviewers/apply, and direct-email invitees from prior manuscripts." (c) `lib/reviewer/actions.ts` — `inviteReviewer` mode='application' server-side gate at line 549 was `if (application.status !== 'active') return error`. Relaxed to `if (application.status === 'declined' || application.status === 'withdrawn') return error`. Without this, dispatching a bucket='approved' entry via mode='application' would have been rejected server-side because the legacy promotion ladder (pending → approved → active) is no longer driven by any UI surface post-Session-32. Inline comment block added to document the rationale for the next engineer reading the file. **Mid-session §6 separation-of-concerns event:** working tree at session-start carried 5 unstaged orphan files from a prior Cowork session that was mid-flight on an editorial-inbox-routing flip (`kanwarparhar@gmail.com` → `oscrsjournal@gmail.com`): `.env.example`, `app/api/submissions/[id]/co-author-dispute/route.ts`, `lib/email/resend.ts`, `lib/email/templates/reviewerApplicationInternalNotification.ts`, `lib/submission/actions.ts`. PLUS a 6th hunk in `lib/reviewer/actions.ts` flipping `INTERNAL_EDITORIAL_EMAIL` between the same two values, which I had to surgically separate from MY own edit on the same file (FUSE `unlink` quirk blocked `git checkout HEAD -- lib/reviewer/actions.ts`, so reverted the orphan hunk in-place via Edit tool, leaving only my mode='application' gate-relax hunk for stage). Confirmed via `git diff` post-revert that only my hunk shipped. Per Kanwar directive at session-end the 5 orphans remain unstaged for the parallel session that owns them. Sweep performed for other surfaces with the same bug pattern: `grep -n listReviewerApplications` turned up only one other call site (`/dashboard/admin/reviewer-applications/page.tsx:69` passing `{ status: 'pending' }`) — that's the intentional use of the legacy column for Applicants-tab triage, not a bug. Stale FUSE locks (`HEAD.lock`, `index.lock`, `tmp_obj_*` × N) cleared via the standard `mv $f .git/.stale-junk/$(basename $f).$(date +%s%N)` workaround per §6 — non-fatal warnings every commit, all completed cleanly. Tree size held at 261. **Live verification via Chrome MCP after Vercel deploy** (~75s post-push): navigated to `https://www.oscrsj.com/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/f8596ce8-2424-4a47-8537-5af9e8a2f5cd?bust=2`; "Reviewer pool (2)" heading confirmed; Manvir Kaur visible with green ACTIVE pill + "1 review submitted" badge + functional Invite button; Rawnak Parhar (kanwarpartap@live.com) visible with green APPROVED pill + functional Invite button. Direct-email reviewers from prior manuscripts now surface in the pool by construction; the Session 32 unified roster is now the single source of truth across both editor surfaces. **Risks worth naming:** (1) **Subspecialty match pill regression for direct-email reviewers.** They have no `reviewer_applications.subspecialty_interests` to match against, so they never get the green "Subspecialty match" pill even if they'd be perfect fits. Acceptable trade-off; the bucket pill (active/approved) carries the more important signal (track record) and editors can read affiliation/country from the metadata line below the name. If a future direct-email panel commit wanted to surface subspecialty manually, it'd need a new optional field on `review_invitations` or a separate "tag a reviewer with subspecialty" admin affordance. (2) **`ReviewerPoolEntry` shape duplicates what `ReviewerRosterEntry` already carries.** Two separate interfaces with overlapping fields is mild type-debt; could consolidate in a future pass by widening `ReviewerRosterEntry` with the optional `subspecialtyInterests` + `careerStage` fields and dropping the separate `ReviewerPoolEntry`. Not done this session to keep the diff tight. (3) **5 prior-session orphan files left unstaged** per §6; flagged to Kanwar; he confirmed parallel session ownership. Until that session lands, every future `git status` will show them — non-fatal but a §6 risk surface. (4) **Session 43's flagged 3 intervening commits (`4aae9f1`, `2290bae`, `5808bae`) STILL not backfilled in CLAUDE.md.** Not addressed this session per §6 separation-of-concerns (those commits weren't this session's work; reconstructing them from `git log` alone risks mis-description). Recommended for the next CEO-led wrap session. **Self-improvement notes for next session's owner-agent:** the Session 32 unified roster rewrite changed bucket semantics but left this and possibly other editor-side surfaces reading the old literal-status column. Worth a defensive sweep next time you touch any reviewer-pool surface: any code that filters `reviewer_applications.status` directly should reference the unified roster bucket logic instead. Same lesson applied to the `inviteReviewer` server-side gate. **Kanwar follow-up actions:** (1) **E2E test the Invite flow** — click "Invite to review" on Manvir's row, set a deadline, fire the invitation; verify a row lands in `review_invitations` with correct snapshot fields and Manvir receives the invitation email. Particularly want to confirm the mode='email' dispatch path works for a reviewer who ALSO has computed bucket='active' (Manvir is the test case). (2) **E2E test the Approved-bucket dispatch** — click "Invite to review" on Rawnak's row; the dispatch goes via mode='application' since he has an applicationId; verify the relaxed server-side gate (now allowing pending/approved/active applications) lets it through without the prior "Only reviewer applications with status 'active' may be invited" error. (3) **Session 44's 3 intervening-commits CLAUDE.md backfill** still pending from Session 43; consider a 5-min Manvir doc-only commit if you want the audit trail tight before the next code-touching session. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 43 — 2026-05-04 — Sushant Cowork — password reset email pipeline unblocked end-to-end: code + Supabase dashboard + 5 email-template token_hash flow  ^session-43-password-reset-email-pipeline

**2026-05-04 Session 43 (Sushant Cowork — password reset email pipeline unblocked end-to-end: code + Supabase dashboard + 5 email-template token_hash flow). Single code commit `3823dcd` on `main` (`5808bae..3823dcd`, fast-forward push, 1 file, +12/-1, `tsc --noEmit` clean, explicit-path stage only).** User-driven debugging session triggered by Kanwar's screenshots — the Reset Password email he received still rendered in the OLD pre-Session-30 cream/peach visual AND clicking the CTA button bounced him to the journal homepage (`/login?error=auth_callback_failed`). Two-bug diagnosis split into a code lane + a dashboard lane, both shipped same session, fully verified end-to-end by Kanwar before wrap-up. **Bug 1 (visual) — diagnosed as Supabase-auth-template territory, NOT our `lib/email/templates/*` Resend shell.** The 5 default Supabase auth emails (Confirm Signup / Invite / Magic Link / Change Email / Reset Password) ship through Supabase's own SMTP via templates configured in Supabase Studio → Authentication → Email Templates — completely separate engine from the Resend templates Sushant Session 30 redesigned. Code can't touch them. Initial fix attempt: handed Kanwar full v2-shell HTML for all 5 templates to paste into the dashboard. He confirmed the new email visual was correct after pasting. **Bug 2 (broken redirect) — root cause: PKCE-flow / server-action cookie mismatch.** `lib/auth/actions.ts:170` `resetPasswordRequest` was passing `redirectTo: ${APP_URL}/reset-password`. Two failure modes stacked: (a) the URL wasn't on the Supabase project's Redirect URLs allowlist, so Supabase silently fell back to the configured Site URL (apex `https://oscrsj.com`) → 307 to `https://www.oscrsj.com` → journal homepage; (b) even with the URL allowlisted, Supabase's default PKCE flow generates a `?code=XXX` link that requires a `code_verifier` cookie to exchange. When `resetPasswordForEmail` runs as a server action, the verifier cookie doesn't reliably make it back to the user's browser, so the code-exchange fails. Per Supabase's own SSR/server-action docs the canonical fix is to use the **token_hash flow** instead, which `app/auth/callback/route.ts` lines 33-44 already implement. **Code commit `3823dcd`:** single 7-line edit + comment block in `lib/auth/actions.ts.resetPasswordRequest` — `redirectTo` flipped from `${APP_URL}/reset-password` → `${APP_URL}/auth/callback?next=/reset-password`. Inline comment block explains both failure modes and the allowlist-vs-token_hash interplay so the next engineer reading the file doesn't trip the same trap. tsc clean. Tree size unchanged. Fast-forward push, explicit-path stage only (`lib/auth/actions.ts`), no `add -A`, no force. CLAUDE.md orphan from Session 42 + the leftover `push-callback-fix.sh` (a prior session's separate-fix script, already shipped) left unstaged per §6 separation-of-concerns. Stale FUSE locks (`HEAD.lock`, `index.lock`, `main.lock`, `tmp_obj_*` × N) cleared mid-session via the standard `mv $f .git/.stale-junk/$(basename $f).$(date +%s%N)` workaround per §6 — non-fatal warnings; commit completed. **Supabase dashboard work (Kanwar executed in URL Configuration):** (1) Site URL flipped from apex `https://oscrsj.com` → canonical `https://www.oscrsj.com` per CLAUDE.md WWW-redirect note; (2) Redirect URLs allowlist gained 3 new entries: `https://www.oscrsj.com/auth/callback`, `https://www.oscrsj.com/reset-password`, `https://www.oscrsj.com/**`. Pre-existing apex `https://oscrsj.com/**` entry left in place (harmless redundancy; apex 307s to www and the site never serves on apex). **5 Supabase email templates updated via Chrome MCP (this session, by Sushant in-browser) to use the token_hash flow URL pattern:** Reset Password (CTA href → `{{ .SiteURL }}/auth/callback?token_hash={{ .TokenHash }}&type=recovery&next=/reset-password`), Confirm Sign Up (`type=signup&next=/dashboard`), Invite User (`type=invite&next=/dashboard`), Magic Link (`type=magiclink&next=/dashboard`), Change Email Address (`type=email_change&next=/dashboard`). **Caught a body-content paste mix-up while editing:** the "Confirm sign up" template had been carrying the "Confirm your new email" body (Kanwar accidentally pasted the wrong v2 HTML into that slot during the earlier paste pass). Replaced wholesale with the correct Confirm Sign Up content + new token_hash href. The other 4 templates were fully rewritten via Monaco `editor.setValue()` for defensive consistency — all 5 now byte-identical pattern except for `<title>`, `<h1>`, body copy paragraph, CTA label, and `type=` URL parameter. Mechanism: `window.monaco.editor.getEditors()[0].setValue(html)` → React state picked up the change (Save button transitioned to enabled green) → click Save → button greyed out (saved). Verified for all 5 templates. **End-to-end verification:** Kanwar requested a fresh `/forgot-password` email post-deploy, clicked the "Reset password" CTA in the email, landed cleanly on `/reset-password` with the password form ready to accept input — no `auth_callback_failed`, no homepage bounce. Kanwar confirmed: "yes it worked." **Risks worth naming:** (1) **Only Reset Password verified end-to-end today** — Confirm Signup, Magic Link, Invite, and Change Email all use the same token_hash flow now and the callback handles them via `verifyOtp({type, token_hash})` (already implemented), but none of those four were click-tested in this session. The 10-minute follow-up retest of all 4 surfaces is overdue from Sessions 5-14. (2) **Site URL apex → www flip changes the `{{ .SiteURL }}` template variable across all 5 emails** — any future Supabase template edit that hardcodes `https://oscrsj.com/...` instead of using `{{ .SiteURL }}/...` would re-introduce the apex-fallback bug; the v2 HTML I shipped uses `{{ .SiteURL }}` consistently in CTA hrefs and footer links so this is defensive by construction, but worth flagging if a future edit pass reaches in to "simplify" any of those URLs. (3) **Subject line / body title alignment not double-checked across all 5 templates** — Invite User's subject is "You've been invited to OSCRSJ" (Supabase-default-style) while the body `<h1>` is "You're invited"; functionally fine but a future polish pass could align them. (4) **The orphan Session 42 CLAUDE.md changelog is bundled into THIS commit's CLAUDE.md backfill** — defensible per the Session 41 / 1b6588e backfill precedent (docs-only, no schema dependency, low rollback cost) but worth flagging that the §6 separation-of-concerns rule was relaxed for the docs lane only. (5) **3 intervening commits between Session 42 and today not yet documented in CLAUDE.md** — `4aae9f1` (Sankalp Lal Section Editor add, Franklin Session 45), `2290bae` (Sankalp Lal headshot wire), `5808bae` (title page template fix, owner unknown from `git log`). The `4aae9f1` Franklin session IS documented in `_Meta/Vault State.md`, but its CLAUDE.md entry is missing. Recommended: separate doc-only backfill commit before the next code-touching session reads CLAUDE.md and gets confused about the recent state. **Self-improvement notes filed in session wrap-up.** **Kanwar follow-up actions (in priority order):** (1) **Optional 10-min smoke test** of the other 4 auth flows now that they all use the token_hash path — sign up a new test account at `/register` (confirm the confirmation email click lands at `/dashboard`); request a magic link if the LoginForm exposes that (or skip — magic link isn't currently surfaced in the UI); change your account email at `/dashboard/settings` (confirm the change-email click lands at `/dashboard`); invite a test user via Supabase Studio → Users → Invite (confirm the invite click lands at `/dashboard`). All four should work mechanically the same as the password reset just did. Caught in `git show --stat HEAD` post-commit (7 files changed, not 6); soft-reset to origin/main, restored CLAUDE.md to unstaged state, recommitted as `ce29350` with exactly the 6 intended files. §6 separation-of-concerns preserved. **The orphan Session 41 CLAUDE.md entry remains in working tree** — flagged to Kanwar for either (i) commit as a doc-only entry rebuilding audit trail for already-shipped commit `e2d093f` (recommended; ~5-min commit), (ii) discard if documented elsewhere, (iii) leave for prior session's owner to resume. **Phase B — Session 41 P0 verification via live Chrome MCP.** (1) **Zero-page Google index — CONFIRMED FALSE NEGATIVE.** Live `site:oscrsj.com` query in actual Google returned at least 16 indexed OSCRSJ pages across 2 SERP pages: homepage, /templates, /media, /faq, /peer-review, /apc, /submit, /aims-scope, /open-access, /editorial-board, /for-reviewers/apply, /guide-for-authors, /articles/in-press, plus 3 news/AI-brief pages. (2) **OSCRJ brand collision — REFINED into three sub-problems.** What Session 41 framed as one finding is actually three: (2a) bare "OSCRSJ" Google search auto-corrects to "OSCARS" (Academy Awards) — Knowledge Graph entity-recognition gap, the highest user-visible severity, and Wikidata entity creation is the unlock once ISSN lands; (2b) full-name unquoted "Orthopedic Surgery Case Reports and Series Journal" search ranks OSCRSJ #1 with OSCRJ + Oxford City Case Reports + Wiley Case Reports in Orthopedics as positions 2-4 (competitor proximity is real but not blocking — visitors who scan headlines may click on the more-established-looking competitor); (2c) AI surfaces — better than Session 41 reported. Live Perplexity test today: direct query "Is OSCRSJ a real journal?" returns positive recognition with the correct full name + oscrsj.com citation + Instagram presence noted ("Yes — OSCRSJ appears to be a real, newly founded orthopedic journal"). The dramatic difference from Session 41's reported ChatGPT/Claude findings is structural — Perplexity is search-augmented (live web fetch with citations), while ChatGPT/Claude lean on training-data corpora that don't yet include OSCRSJ but do include OSCRJ. **As OSCRSJ accumulates indexed content + age, those surfaces catch up — content depth + content age, not schema, is the unlock.** Open-ended "best open-access ortho case report journals" Perplexity query does NOT surface OSCRSJ — content-depth gap, not schema; gates on first published articles. Manvir handoff `^handoff-oscrj-brand-collision-decision-2026-05-03` REFINED with the three-way nuance + recommendation to defer Option A (on-page disambiguation paragraph) until ISSN lands (lower amplification risk pre-ISSN), approve Option B (alternateName JSON-LD enumeration) as immediate ship, lock Option C (Wikidata) as ISSN-gated milestone. New Sushant inbound handoff `^handoff-organization-alternatename-enumeration-2026-05-03` filed end-of-session — ~5-line schema edit on `app/layout.tsx` to populate `Organization.alternateName` array; target 2026-05-17. **Anomalies surfaced for opportunistic cleanup (none P0):** (1) `/peer-review` and `/news/ai-in-orthopedics/research-tools` are indexed under apex `oscrsj.com` not www form — Sprint 1 canonical fix (Session 39 commit `5b6d5dd`) hasn't propagated yet, expected to resolve organically by 2026-05-15 per Sprint 1's wrap notes. Collateral copy fixes propagated to `app/templates/page.tsx` (pre-flight checklist + Format Requirements row) and `app/guide-for-authors/page.tsx` (Formatting block + new running-title / superscript-citation / References-placement rules) so author-facing docs stop describing the old italic-heading convention. Verification: round-tripped each template via python-docx — all 6 round-trip clean; XML spot-check confirms 0 ALL-CAPS headings, 3 superscript hyperlinks per template, 3 bookmarks per template, 3 page breaks per template (Intro + References + Figure legends), centered References + Figure legends headings, right-aligned header on every section. 9 files changed (+686/-6). **Risks worth naming (backfill-inferred):** (1) authors who downloaded a template before this commit are working off the old italic-heading convention; manuscripts already in flight may need a one-line Operations note from Manvir's next outreach pass to prompt a re-download. (2) Build-script-as-source-of-truth means any future single-template tweak should run through `python3 scripts/build-article-templates.py` rather than direct `.docx` edit, otherwise re-runs of the script revert the change.

### Session 39 — 2026-04-30 — Franklin Cowork — Sprint 1 SEO bundle: site-wide canonical sweep + soft-404 guards on `/articles` + `/articles/in-press`  ^session-39-sprint-1-seo-bundle

**2026-04-30 Session 39 (Franklin Cowork — Sprint 1 SEO bundle: site-wide canonical sweep + soft-404 guards on `/articles` + `/articles/in-press`). Single commit `5b6d5dd` on `main` (`19bbf89..5b6d5dd`).** Closes 3 inbound John handoffs in one batch per Kanwar's Sprint 1 directive: `^handoff-site-wide-canonical-sweep` (filed 2026-04-24) + `^handoff-soft-404-guard-articles-filter` (filed 2026-04-24) + `^handoff-soft-404-guard-in-press` (filed 2026-04-25). 25 files changed, +470/-28. **(1) Canonical sweep:** 22 indexable routes gained explicit `alternates.canonical` (www-form) + description + `openGraph` block — `/`, `/aims-scope`, `/apc`, `/contact`, `/editorial-board`, `/editorial-policies`, `/for-reviewers`, `/for-reviewers/apply`, `/media`, `/open-access`, `/peer-review`, `/privacy`, `/register`, `/submit`, `/subscribe`, `/terms`, `/articles`, `/articles/in-press`, plus canonical+OG appended to `/accessibility`, `/faq`, `/guide-for-authors` (already had descriptions). Pattern: each metadata export expands `{title}` → `{title, description, alternates:{canonical:'<absolute-www-url>'}, openGraph:{title, description, url, type:'website'}}`. Title cleanup dropped redundant `— OSCRSJ` suffix on every touched page so the root layout's `%s | OSCRSJ` template stops double-suffixing. **(2) `/articles` soft-404 guard:** `app/articles/page.tsx` was already a server component (Manvir, `93f6c9d`) so John's specced refactor was unnecessary. Replaced the static `metadata` export with `generateMetadata({searchParams})` running `count='exact', head: true` against `manuscripts WHERE status='published' AND subspecialty=<mapped-internal-slug>`; emits `robots: { index: false, follow: true }` when the topic filter has 0 matches. `URL_TO_INTERNAL_SLUG` map (`hand-wrist → hand`, `sports-medicine → sports`, `pediatric-orthopedics → pediatrics`, `orthopedic-oncology → tumor`) hoisted into the function so the count query matches the actual `subspecialty` DB column. Canonical always points at bare `/articles` regardless of filter — filter variants are UX discovery aids, not canonical pages. **(3) `/articles/in-press` soft-404 guard + JSON-LD:** same conditional-noindex pattern (count `manuscripts WHERE status='in_production'`, noindex while 0); flips to `index: true, follow: true` automatically as soon as the first manuscript reaches in_production. Title dropped its `— OSCRSJ` suffix per the same template rule. `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD always rendered (Home → Articles → In Press); conditional `ItemList` of `ScholarlyArticle` nodes only when `articles.length > 0` (Google warns on empty ItemList). Each `ScholarlyArticle` carries `headline`, optional `identifier` (DOI URL when present), optional `dateAccepted`, `author[]` (Person nodes with optional `sameAs` ORCID), and `isPartOf: { '@id': 'https://www.oscrsj.com/#periodical' }` linking back to the journal Periodical declared in `app/layout.tsx`. JSON-LD blocks mounted as inline `<script type="application/ld+json">` matching the existing `/peer-review` FAQPage pattern. **Defense-in-depth bundled:** `robots: { index: false, follow: false }` on `app/dashboard/layout.tsx` — transitive cascade across every `/dashboard/*` route including `/dashboard/admin/*` via layout inheritance. Replaces the per-route noindex pattern; cleaner and survives any future routing change that briefly exposes a dashboard URL. ### Session 36b — 2026-04-27 — Sushant Cowork — first-revision wizard gate fix, follow-on to Session 36  ^session-36b-first-revision-wizard-gate-fix

**2026-04-27 Session 36b (Sushant Cowork — first-revision wizard gate fix, follow-on to Session 36). Single commit on `main`.** Same-day E2E smoke test by Kanwar surfaced that opening `/dashboard/submit?revising={id}` on a manuscript with a freshly-issued Minor or Major Revisions decision rendered the BASE submission upload list (Main Manuscript / Blinded Manuscript / Title Page / Tables / Figures / Supplementary Materials / Cover Letter / Ethics Approval) instead of the REVISION upload list (Revised Manuscript / Revised Blinded / Tracked-Changes Manuscript [required] / Revised Title Page / Revised Tables / Response to Reviewers [required] / Revised Figures / Ethics Approval). Latent off-by-one bug shipped in Session 12 and untouched ever since: `Step2Files.tsx` gates revising-mode rendering on `typeof revisionNumber === 'number' && revisionNumber >= 2`, but `loadRevisionContext` (in `lib/submission/actions.ts:1500`) returns `revisionNumber: priorCount + 1` where `priorCount` counts existing `manuscript_revisions` rows. For the FIRST revision (priorCount = 0), `revisionNumber` evaluates to 1, and the `>= 2` gate is false — so Step2Files falls back to BASE_CATEGORIES even though `SubmissionWizard.tsx`'s own `isRevising = !!revisionContext` (truthy check) had correctly entered revising mode. Compounded by a second off-by-one: `currentVersion = isRevising ? revisionNumber! : 1` was identity-mapping the revision_number → file version, but the canonical convention (documented in CLAUDE.md Session 14 RevisionsPanel comment block) is `manuscript_files.version = revision_number + 1` because v=1 is reserved for the original submission, v=2 = first revision, v=3 = second revision, etc. The bug therefore would have ALSO clobbered v1 originals on upload because in revising mode the wizard would have written new `manuscript_files` rows at version=1 alongside the original v1 set, bypassing the prior-version-hide filter `currentVersionFiles.filter(f => f.version === currentVersion)` that's supposed to keep the v1 originals invisible during revision uploads. **Fix:** single 7-line change in `app/dashboard/submit/Step2Files.tsx` — gate is now `typeof revisionNumber === 'number' && revisionNumber >= 1` (presence-based + value sanity), and `currentVersion = isRevising ? revisionNumber! + 1 : 1` (so first revision lands at v2, second at v3, etc.). Inline comment block added to document the off-by-one for the next engineer who reads the file. **Coverage check:** the Storage path computation `manuscripts/${id}/v${currentVersion}/${storageName}` and the prior-version-hide filter both correctly key off `currentVersion`, so once the bug is fixed both surfaces line up automatically — no other call sites needed to change. `manuscript_files.version` written via `recordFile()` already accepts `version` as a parameter, so this is purely a client-side mapping fix. `tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0). Tree size 239 → 239 (no new files). **Why this didn't surface earlier:** Phase 3 (Session 12) shipped revising mode without an E2E test of an actual first-revision flow because no test manuscript had reached `revision_requested` status yet. Sessions 13-35 added editorial decision composer, decision rescind, batch decisions, revisions panel, AI disclosure, etc. — all on the editor side. Session 36 (this one) was the first to ship a real revision-author surface (the response template + email instructions), and the fix happened on the immediate retest. **Risks worth naming:** (1) **No second-revision regression.** With `revisionNumber >= 1`, second revisions (revisionNumber=2, currentVersion=3) and third revisions (revisionNumber=3, currentVersion=4) all flow through the same revising-mode branch. Schema/Storage are unchanged. (2) **Pre-fix-deploy in-flight revisions.** Any author who started a revision between the Session 36 push and this fix-deploy and uploaded files via Step2Files would have written `manuscript_files.version=1` rows, polluting the v1 record. At pre-launch the count is likely 0 — Kanwar's E2E test was the first attempt. If the count > 0, the cleanup is a manual `DELETE FROM manuscript_files WHERE version=1 AND created_at > '<session 36 ship time>'` after eyeballing which rows are accidental. None expected. **Kanwar follow-up actions:** (1) Vercel auto-deploys ~60s; reload `/dashboard/submit?revising={id}` (Cmd+Shift+R if cached). The page header should now read "Upload Revised Files (v2)" and the upload list should show 8 REVISION_CATEGORIES tiles with Tracked-Changes Manuscript and Response to Reviewers carrying red asterisks (required). The original v1 files (TFD Final.docx, Title.docx, Table 1.docx, etc.) should disappear from the list because the filter is now keyed on v2 — that's the intended behavior; v1 files stay on record in `manuscript_files` for audit. (2) Re-test the upload — drop a `.docx` into the Tracked-Changes Manuscript slot, confirm it lands at Storage path `manuscripts/{id}/v2/{id}_v2_tracked_changes_1.docx` and the `manuscript_files` row has `version=2`. (3) When Submit Revision fires, `submitRevision` validates that `manuscript_files` has rows with `file_type IN ('manuscript', 'blinded_manuscript', 'tracked_changes', 'response_to_reviewers')` — version-agnostic — so it'll succeed. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 34 — 2026-04-26 — Sushant Cowork — orphan migration 012 committed, editorial-decision submission unblocked  ^session-34-orphan-migration-012-committed

**2026-04-26 Session 34 (Sushant Cowork — orphan migration 012 committed, editorial-decision submission unblocked). Single commit on `main`.** Triggered by Kanwar reporting `Failed to record decision: Could not find the 'pre_revision_snapshot' column of 'editorial_decisions' in the schema cache` when clicking Submit Decision (Accept) on `/dashboard/admin/manuscripts/[id]`. Root cause matched the Session 29 flag exactly: `supabase/migrations/012_pre_revision_snapshots.sql` had been sitting on disk untracked since Session 15 (Session 15's CLAUDE.md entry mistakenly listed it as shipped; Sessions 12-14's revising flow + the Session 14 RevisionsPanel + the `submitEditorialDecision` capture path in `lib/admin/actions.ts:572-597` all reference it) but had never been committed to git AND had never been run in Supabase production. Kanwar's `submitEditorialDecision` always writes `pre_revision_snapshot` on the INSERT — value is `null` for Accept/Reject/Desk-Reject, populated only for Minor/Major Revisions per the design comment block — so even an Accept decision tripped the schema-cache miss. **Fix:** Kanwar pasted the migration's column-add statements (7 ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS — 1 on editorial_decisions for `pre_revision_snapshot jsonb`, 6 on manuscript_revisions for `snapshot_title text` / `snapshot_abstract text` / `snapshot_keywords text[]` / `snapshot_subspecialty text` / `snapshot_authors jsonb` / `snapshot_source text` / `snapshot_captured_at timestamptz`) into the Supabase SQL Editor with a trailing `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` so PostgREST refreshed its cache immediately rather than waiting for the ~10-minute auto-refresh. Kanwar confirmed unblock. **Commit:** explicit-path stage of the long-orphaned `supabase/migrations/012_pre_revision_snapshots.sql` (128 lines, untouched from disk — same DDL Kanwar pasted, no rewrite needed since prod schema now matches the file byte-for-byte) + this CLAUDE.md changelog entry. No code change. No schema drift between repo and prod. Tree size 235 → 236 (+1: migration 012). Fast-forward push, explicit-path stage only, no `add -A`, no force. **Risks worth naming:** (1) **Pre-existing minor/major editorial_decisions rows carry `pre_revision_snapshot = NULL`** — the migration's advisory NOTICE block surfaces the count of legacy rows; the future field-level diff UI honestly renders "snapshot unavailable" rather than reconstruct from post-revision state per the migration's design comment. At pre-launch volume this is likely 0 or low single digits. (2) **Other long-orphaned migrations may exist** — Session 29's flag was specifically about 012; a belt-and-suspenders sweep would diff every `supabase/migrations/*.sql` against `git ls-files` to confirm all are committed. Not done this session — flagging for any future session that wants to defend against the same failure mode in one shot. (3) **PostgREST schema cache refresh is asynchronous by default** — Kanwar's `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` made the cache pick up the columns immediately, but any future migration that adds columns referenced by application code should end with the same NOTIFY to avoid the same ~10-minute window of cache-miss errors. **Kanwar follow-up actions:** (1) Already complete — migration ran, decision submission unblocked. (2) Optional belt + suspenders: re-test the Issue Decision flow on a Minor or Major Revisions decision (not just Accept) to confirm the snapshot is captured — look for a non-null `pre_revision_snapshot` jsonb payload on the new `editorial_decisions` row via Supabase Table Editor; verify the payload has `title/abstract/keywords/subspecialty/authors[]` per the schema in the migration's comment block. (3) Optional: 5-min sweep of `supabase/migrations/` vs. `git ls-files supabase/migrations/` to surface any other untracked migrations sitting on disk. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 31 — 2026-04-26 — Sushant Cowork — combined reviewer manuscript package on acceptance  ^session-31-combined-reviewer-manuscript-package

**2026-04-26 Session 31 (Sushant Cowork — combined reviewer manuscript package on acceptance). Single commit on `main`.** Triggered by Kanwar's directive after a real reviewer accepted an invitation: when a reviewer accepts, automatically email them a single combined Word document containing the blinded manuscript, the tables document (if any), and each figure on its own page with a caption. Closes the long-standing UX gap where accepting reviewers landed at `/review/[token]/manuscript` with a list of separate files to download (blinded.docx + tables.docx + N figure images) and had to assemble the package mentally. **Architecture:** new module `lib/reviewer-package/build.ts` (732 lines) that opens `blinded_manuscript.docx` with `pizzip`, splices the body content of `tables.docx` into it (extract everything between `<w:body>` and the trailing `<w:sectPr>`, inject before the base doc's `sectPr` via regex match-and-slice — preserves the base manuscript's section properties; tables.docx's are dropped because two `sectPr` blocks corrupt layout), then appends a page-break + image-paragraph + caption-paragraph for each figure. Image binaries land in `word/media/`, relationships are appended to `word/_rels/document.xml.rels` (rId allocator scans existing IDs and starts from max+1 to avoid collisions), and `[Content_Types].xml` gains `<Default Extension="png|jpeg|gif|tiff">` declarations only if not already present. Image dimensions are read directly from the binary (PNG header at offset 16, JPEG SOFn marker scan, GIF LSD at offset 6) and scaled aspect-preserving to a 6-inch target width in EMU (914400 EMU = 1 inch); TIFF dims fall back to 6×4 inches because TIFF dim parsing isn't worth the complexity for the rare case where Word can't render TIFF anyway. Caption format: `Figure {n} — {original_filename}`, italic, centered, beneath the image. **Cache contract:** keyed by `(manuscript_id, current_version)` where `current_version = max(manuscript_revisions.revision_number) + 1` (or 1 for never-revised manuscripts). Three new columns on `manuscript_metadata` per **migration 017** (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS text|timestamptz|integer`): `reviewer_package_storage_path` (the cached .docx path, NULL until first successful build), `reviewer_package_built_at` (sentinel timestamp set BEFORE work begins to prevent concurrent builds — second caller within 90s sees "in progress" and bails), `reviewer_package_version` (which manuscript-version the cached file matches). When a revision is submitted, `current_version` bumps and the next reviewer accepting on the new version triggers a fresh build; old cache file remains in Storage until manually pruned (low priority, manuscripts/ paths are namespaced by version). **Storage layout:** `manuscripts/{id}/v{n}/reviewer-package.docx`, same convention as the original blinded files. **Email delivery:** new template `lib/email/templates/reviewerManuscriptPackage.ts` rendered via the dark-brown brand shell shipped Session 30. Subject `Manuscript package for review — {submission_id} — OSCRSJ`. Two delivery modes decided by package size: **attached** if raw bytes ≤ 22 MB (leaves headroom under Resend's 40 MB cap after base64 inflation ~33%), or **link-only** with a 14-day signed Storage URL (matches the 21-day review deadline minus a buffer for late starters). `lib/email/resend.ts` extended with optional `attachments?: SendEmailAttachment[]` on `SendEmailParams` (filename + Buffer content + optional contentType) — backward-compatible, every existing caller still works without change. **Wire-up in `acceptReviewInvitation` in `lib/reviewer/actions.ts`:** new private helper `sendReviewerPackageEmail(invitation, manuscript)` runs AFTER the existing acceptance-confirmation + editor-notification emails so even if the build hits the function-timeout ceiling on a heavy manuscript, the reviewer already has confirmation that their acceptance was recorded. Build runs synchronously inside the action — no fire-and-forget pattern because Next.js 14 server actions die when the response is sent and `unstable_after()` only landed in Next 15. On success: re-downloads bytes from Storage (cache-hit case has no bytes in hand), decides attach-vs-link, fires the package email, audit-logs `reviewer_package_built` (first build) or `reviewer_package_email_sent` (cache hit) with `{version, bytes, delivery_mode, cached, figure_count, has_tables}`. On failure (build error, sentinel-says-in-progress, etc.): fires a fallback email to the reviewer pointing at `/review/[token]/manuscript` for per-file downloads, fires an editorial-team alert email to `INTERNAL_EDITORIAL_EMAIL` (Kanwar's Gmail until Workspace) so he can intervene if needed, audit-logs `reviewer_package_build_failed`. **Reviewer manuscript page surface upgrade** at `app/review/[token]/manuscript/page.tsx`: when the cache exists, renders a prominent peach-on-brown-dark CTA card at the top of the page ("Combined manuscript package · One Word document, end-to-end") with a 30-min signed download URL + size label; per-file list demoted to "Individual files (fallback)" beneath. When cache is empty (build still running for first reviewer), renders a "Your package is being prepared" placeholder. The per-file `REVIEWER_SAFE_FILE_TYPES` allowlist in the existing `getReviewerFileSignedUrl` action was widened to include `tables` (was `['blinded_manuscript','figure','supplement']`) so reviewers can also download tables.docx individually as a fallback. **Smoke testing in sandbox:** wrote two CommonJS test scripts that round-tripped through pizzip — the body-splice path (blinded body + tables body + page-break separator + restored sectPr at end, generates a valid 1.3KB .docx that re-opens cleanly) and the figure-embed path (1×1 PNG added to `word/media/`, rId2 relationship appended, `<Default Extension="png">` content-type declaration added, drawing element with proper EMU dims spliced into body before sectPr, generates a valid 1.9KB .docx with image binary + caption preserved). Both round-trip checks confirmed the generated zips are well-formed and the spliced XML survives a fresh `new PizZip(generated).file('word/document.xml').asText()` read. Test artifacts saved to `outputs/smoke-{output,figure}.docx` for manual Word inspection. **Code changes (9 files):** new `supabase/migrations/017_reviewer_package_cache.sql` (45 lines), new `lib/reviewer-package/build.ts` (732 lines — the meat), new `lib/email/templates/reviewerManuscriptPackage.ts` (169 lines), modified `lib/reviewer/actions.ts` (+271/-9, mostly the new `sendReviewerPackageEmail` helper + `tables` added to `REVIEWER_SAFE_FILE_TYPES`), `lib/email/resend.ts` (+25/-2 for attachments support), `lib/types/database.ts` (+8 lines for the 3 new metadata columns), `app/review/[token]/manuscript/page.tsx` (+94/-2 for the package CTA card), `package.json` (+1 — `pizzip ^3.2.0`), `package-lock.json` (+16). +1352/-9 combined. Tree size 229 → 232 (+3: migration + build module + email template). `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean (one downlevelIteration error on `for…of Set` was caught + fixed via `Array.from(extensions)` before commit). Fast-forward push, explicit-path stage only (the 9 files above), no `add -A`, no force. Stale FUSE locks (`index.lock` + `tmp_obj_*` warnings) cleared mid-session via the standard `mv $f .git/.stale-junk/$(basename $f).$(date +%s%N)` workaround per §6. **Risks worth naming:** (1) **Vercel Hobby timeout.** Build runs inline in the acceptance server action; Hobby tier is 10s. A manuscript with 6+ high-res TIFFs may push past during the first reviewer's accept. Mitigation in code: the sentinel pattern means concurrent reviewers don't compete; mitigation operationally: move to Vercel Pro (60s) or shift the build to a cron-driven background queue when first hit. (2) **TIFF embed quality.** Word for Mac renders TIFF inconsistently; Word for Windows + LibreOffice handle it cleanly. We pass TIFFs through unmodified rather than converting to PNG (would have required Sharp dep + extra build time). If reviewers flag TIFF rendering issues, a Sharp-based TIFF→PNG transcode is the right follow-up. (3) **Body-splice fidelity.** Tables.docx custom styles, numbering, or footnotes that don't exist in the base manuscript render as plain text in the merged doc. Acceptable for reviewer reading; would matter more for editorial production. (4) **Storage cleanup.** Old cached packages (from prior versions) are not auto-deleted on revision. Low priority — packages are small (typically <10 MB) and Storage is cheap. **Kanwar follow-up actions (in priority order):** (1) **Run migration 017 in Supabase SQL Editor** — three idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` statements, no enum gotchas, verify with `SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_name = 'manuscript_metadata' AND column_name LIKE 'reviewer_package_%'` returning 3 rows. **Until 017 runs, the build will fail in production** because Supabase will reject UPDATE/INSERT to non-existent columns; the failure will trip the fallback email path (reviewer gets a "manuscript files ready" link to /review/[token]/manuscript instead of the combined package). (2) **Smoke test the full path** — issue a fresh test invitation to your own email on a manuscript with a real blinded MS + tables + 1-2 figures, click Accept on `/review/[token]`, then check your inbox for **two emails**: the existing acceptance receipt ("Your review acceptance has been recorded") + the new package email ("Manuscript package for review — {id}"). The package email should attach the .docx if total size <22 MB, otherwise contain a "Download package" button linking to a 14-day signed URL. Open the .docx in Word/Pages and verify the body reads start-to-finish: manuscript body → page break → "TABLES" heading → tables → page break → Figure 1 with caption → page break → Figure 2 with caption → etc. (3) **Inspect `email_logs`** after the test — should see two new rows: one `reviewer_invitation_accepted_confirmation` + one `reviewer_manuscript_package` (or `reviewer_package_fallback` if build errored). Both should show `delivery_status = 'sent'` and a non-null `resend_message_id`. (4) **Inspect `audit_logs`** — should see `invitation_accepted` + either `reviewer_package_built` (first build) or `reviewer_package_email_sent` (cache hit). Failures show as `reviewer_package_build_failed` with the error captured in `details.error`. (5) **Second reviewer cache test** — if you invite a second test reviewer to the same manuscript and they accept, the package email should arrive within ~2 sec instead of 5-8 (cache hit, no rebuild). (6) **Revision invalidation test** (only if you want belt-and-suspenders) — submit a revision on a manuscript that has a cached package, invite a reviewer to the revised version, confirm a fresh package is built with the revised files. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 29b — 2026-04-26 — Sushant Cowork — AI disclosure now requires explicit choice  ^session-29b-ai-disclosure-now-requires

**2026-04-26 Session 29b (Sushant Cowork — AI disclosure now requires explicit choice). Single commit on `main`.** Same-day E2E retest follow-up after Session 29's reviewer-export ship + Kanwar's Step 3 confirmation. Kanwar surfaced that Step 5's AI-Assisted Writing Disclosure was a single optional checkbox: unchecked = silently treated as "no AI used" with no explicit affirmation, which doesn't meet the editorial intent of forcing every author to actively disclose. **Fix:** swapped the checkbox for a required radio pair — "I did not use AI writing tools..." (false) and "AI writing tools were used..." (true) — with neither pre-selected on a fresh draft. **Tri-state in client wizard state:** `WizardState.aiToolsUsed: boolean | null` where `null` means "author has not yet picked either radio". Step 5 completion (`step5Complete`) and the Submit button stay disabled until the author explicitly clicks one of the radios; if they pick "yes", the description textarea is also required as before. **Resume-from-draft handling:** `initialStateFromDraft` only seeds `aiToolsUsed = true` when there is positive evidence the author previously chose "yes" (`meta.ai_tools_used === true` OR `meta.ai_tools_details` non-empty); every other case (no metadata row, or `ai_tools_used` is the column default-false with no details) seeds `null` so the radio is unselected and the editor must explicitly answer. **`saveDeclarations` no longer writes `ai_tools_used` + `ai_tools_details` columns when the client passes `null`** — this was the critical bug fix: previously every auto-save (including from Steps 1-4) was writing `ai_tools_used: false` to `manuscript_metadata`, which created a positive-looking "no" answer in the DB before the author had even seen Step 5. With the new tri-state, the columns retain their NOT-NULL DEFAULT false on first row creation but the wizard never claims that as a chosen answer until the author explicitly clicks. **Review summary copy** updated to render a red "Not yet answered — pick one of the radio options above" line when `aiToolsUsed === null`. **Files touched (3):** `app/dashboard/submit/Step5Declarations.tsx` (UI swap + interface widened to `aiToolsUsed: boolean | null` + tri-state validation `aiAnswered + aiDetailsComplete` + review summary tri-state branch); `app/dashboard/submit/SubmissionWizard.tsx` (`WizardState.aiToolsUsed: boolean | null` + revised `initialStateFromDraft` seeding logic + `step5Complete` gates on `aiAnswered && aiDetailsOk`); `lib/submission/actions.ts` (`saveDeclarations.aiToolsUsed: boolean | null` + skip-write-when-null branch). +108/-33 lines. **No new migration, no new env vars, no new routes.** `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean. (2) **Verify resume behavior** — for any draft you've edited in Sessions 1-29 (with `meta.ai_tools_used = false` from the prior auto-save default), opening the wizard now should show neither radio pre-selected; the wizard re-prompts you to make an explicit choice. This is the desired outcome — the prior default-false was masquerading as an answered "no". (4) **Migration 012 status check** — log into Supabase prod and run `SELECT column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_name = 'manuscript_revisions' AND column_name LIKE 'snapshot_%'` (also `editorial_decisions.pre_revision_snapshot`); if rows return, migration 012's schema is live and a future Sushant session just needs to commit the file as-is; if zero rows return, the revising flow has been silently broken and migration 012 needs to run. **Handoffs pushed: None.**

### Session 28 — 2026-04-26 — Sushant Cowork — E2E test bug triage  ^session-28-e2e-test-bug-triage

**2026-04-26 Session 28 (Sushant Cowork — E2E test bug triage) — title-page + tables upload pipeline unblocked end-to-end. Two commits on `main`: `bb44ea2` + `554f798`, plus migration 015 capturing the live-applied prod fix.** Mid-test support session triggered by Kanwar's E2E walkthrough of the case-report submission flow. Four issues surfaced and resolved: **(1)** Tables wording: label `Tables Document` → `Tables`. **(2)** Figures format question: Kanwar verbally directed converting figures to multi-file `.docx`; reverted after analysis confirmed the canonical `/templates` + `/guide-for-authors` design (single `Tables.docx` + image-file figures at ≥300 DPI) is what every external surface promises authors AND is materially easier for the renderer (one Pandoc call for tables; image files preserve DPI/color profile and match the JATS XML `<graphic xlink:href="…"/>` shape PubMed expects). **(3)** Title-page upload red error box: root cause was the `manuscript_files.file_type` enum — Postgres didn't yet have `'title_page'` (or `'tables'`) values committed, so DB inserts failed and Storage objects orphaned. **(4)** Tables upload "Failed to upload Table 1.docx: The resource already exists": same orphan + a separate Storage-RLS gap surfaced post-`upsert: true` fix. **`bb44ea2` (initial fix attempt):** rewrote `Tables Document` → `Tables` and `Figures (image files)` → `Figures` labels in `Step2Files.tsx` BASE_CATEGORIES + REVISION_CATEGORIES; rewrote `Step5Declarations.tsx` `FILE_TYPE_LABELS` map (`tables: 'Tables Document'` → `tables: 'Tables'`); switched `supabase.storage.upload(...)` from `upsert: false` → `upsert: true` so retries silently overwrite an orphaned object instead of 409'ing with "The resource already exists"; added `await admin.storage.from('submissions').remove([storagePath])` in `recordFile()` server action's DB-insert-failure path so future enum/insert errors don't accumulate ghosts on the bucket. Also (the part that got reverted) bumped `tables.maxFiles` 1→10 + flipped `figure.accept` from `.jpeg/.jpg/.png/.tiff/.tif` → `.docx`, with parallel changes to REVISION_CATEGORIES — this conflicted with the canonical `/templates` design described above. **`554f798` (revert of design half, kept wording + resilience):** restored `tables.maxFiles: 1` + canonical "single `Tables.docx`, one table per page, real Word tables, caption above each" description; restored `figure.accept` to image formats + canonical "300 DPI, separate image file per figure, do NOT embed in manuscript or Word document" description; same restorations on REVISION_CATEGORIES. Net code change after both commits: 3 files modified (`app/dashboard/submit/Step2Files.tsx`, `app/dashboard/submit/Step5Declarations.tsx`, `lib/submission/actions.ts`); +60/-23 lines combined; no new routes; no new env vars. **Manual Kanwar actions completed in Supabase prod (mid-session)** — these were the actual unblockers, the code changes alone weren't sufficient: **(a) migration 014** (file_type enum extension shipped Session 19) was finally run end-to-end. First attempt (paste all three statements + run) errored with PG **`55P04` "unsafe use of new value 'title_page' of enum type file_type"** because Supabase SQL Editor wraps multi-statement runs in a single transaction and PG forbids referencing a newly-added enum value in the same txn that added it (even via `enum_range`). Workaround: ran each `ALTER TYPE file_type ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS '<v>'` as its own query, then verified via the catalog read `SELECT enumlabel FROM pg_enum WHERE enumtypid = 'file_type'::regtype` (catalog reads dodge 55P04 because they don't materialize the new value as a typed value). Both `'title_page'` and `'tables'` confirmed present in production enum. **(b)** manually deleted every storage object in the test draft's `{manuscriptId}/v1/` folder — these were orphans from earlier failed attempts (Storage write succeeded, DB insert failed pre-migration-014, Storage object remained). **(c)** **created a new UPDATE policy on `storage.objects`** for the `'submissions'` bucket — surfaced as the second-stage blocker after `upsert: true`. The bucket had INSERT, SELECT, and DELETE policies but **no UPDATE policy**; Supabase Storage's `upsert: true` path falls through to UPDATE when the target object already exists, and PG denied the UPDATE for lack of policy → "new row violates row-level security policy" (the same generic error PG emits for both INSERT-WITH-CHECK and UPDATE-WITH-CHECK violations). The new policy mirrors the existing INSERT policy shape (same loose `bucket_id = 'submissions'` check, same `authenticated` role, no path/filename pattern matching). Captured in **`supabase/migrations/015_storage_objects_update_policy.sql`** (idempotent via `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + `CREATE POLICY`) so a future project rebuild reproduces the policy from source. **Verified end-to-end by Kanwar after the three manual actions:** title page + tables uploads now succeed; resolution applies universally across all article types (Case Report / Case Series / Surgical Technique / Images in Orthopedics / Letter to the Editor / Review Article) because (i) the file_type enum is single-typed, type-agnostic; (ii) the storage policy is bucket-level, not file_type-aware; (iii) `Step2Files.tsx` uses a single shared `BASE_CATEGORIES` array with no manuscript-type branching. **Franklin sweep handoff** `^handoff-tables-figures-consistency-sweep-2026-04-26` pushed mid-session, **closed same-day by Franklin in commit `c1fdcbf`** (afternoon Cowork session — 2 stragglers fixed: REVISION_CATEGORIES tables description still said "Updated Tables document if tables changed during revision" because Sushant's revert aligned BASE_CATEGORIES but missed the parallel REVISION path; CLAUDE.md Session 19 historical entry still said `tables: 'Tables Document'` for the FILE_TYPE_LABELS map — corrected to current code state `tables: 'Tables'`). Tree size 225 → 226 (+1 migration file). `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean both Sushant commits. Fast-forward pushes `ea2762c..bb44ea2..554f798`, explicit-path stage only, no `add -A`, no force. Stale FUSE locks (`HEAD.lock`, `index.lock`, `objects/maintenance.lock`, multi-second-old `tmp_obj_*` files) cleared mid-session via `mv $f .git/.stale-junk/$(basename $f).$(date +%s%N)` per §6 — non-fatal warnings on every commit, all completed cleanly. **Open polish (non-blocking, Kanwar discretion):** the wizard renders the Tables + Figures slots for every article type, but `/guide-for-authors` says Letters to the Editor and Images in Orthopedics shouldn't have tables (Letters: max 1 figure-or-table; Images: figure-driven only, 0 tables). The slot is "If applicable" (not required) so authors of those types simply leave it empty — but if Kanwar ever wants the wizard to hide the Tables/Figures slots for types that don't permit them per the spec, that's a small `Step2Files.tsx` change that gates the BASE_CATEGORIES array on `manuscriptType`. **Kanwar follow-up actions:** (1) migration 015 needs to be run on production Supabase IF Kanwar wants the source-of-truth to match — actually NO: the policy was already created live via SQL Editor with the same DDL the migration emits, so re-running migration 015 in prod is a no-op (the `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` makes it idempotent). The migration is for *reproducibility on rebuild*, not a pending action. (2) Optional polish above. (3) End-to-end auth retest still pending from Sessions 5–14. **Handoffs pushed: Franklin** (`^handoff-tables-figures-consistency-sweep-2026-04-26` — closed same-day in `c1fdcbf`).

### 2026-04-23 — SEO follow-up (post-ship GSC triage)  ^entry-2026-04-23-seo-follow-up-post-ship-gsc

**2026-04-23 SEO follow-up (post-ship GSC triage)** — After `2ade2d8` shipped, ran GSC Live Test on the 4 former topic slugs to pre-empt the 404 emails. `/topics/sports-medicine` → ✅ URL is available to Google → Request Indexing accepted. `/topics/hand-wrist` → ❌ **Soft 404** because the redirect target `/articles?topic=hand-wrist` renders 0 matching articles (sample set only has 3 articles, covering sports-medicine / trauma / foot-ankle) — Google's Soft 404 detector flags empty-filter views as 404-equivalents. Same pattern will hit `/topics/pediatric-orthopedics` + `/topics/orthopedic-oncology`. **Soft 404s on the 3 sibling URLs are non-blocking** — they self-resolve as soon as each subspecialty gets a real article, and sitemap refetch already removed them from the active crawl queue. But the systemic implication is broader — **any empty filter state on `/articles?topic=<slug>` will Soft-404 until that topic has ≥1 article**. Handoff `^handoff-per-page-seo-acceptance-criteria` pushed to John (2026-04-23) with 4 deliverables: (1) near-term fix for empty-filter Soft 404 via richer empty state OR per-request `generateMetadata` noindex, (2) publish "Per-Page SEO Acceptance Criteria" checklist at [[Technical SEO Pipeline]], (3) codify as an Organization Plan §7.7 brief-authoring gate so every new route ships SEO-correct by construction, (4) retroactive audit of ~30 public-indexable routes. Paired with the existing `^handoff-canonical-metadata-audit-sitewide` — same per-page walk; canonical tag is a line-item on the broader checklist. Sitemap resubmitted successfully in GSC the same session; Google will refetch and see the new 37-URL set. **Future engineers touching `/articles`: if you reintroduce filter-based URLs, they must either (a) guarantee ≥1 indexed result in every category or (b) emit per-request `robots: { index: false }` on empty states, or Google will Soft-404 the empty views. See John's acceptance criteria at [[Technical SEO Pipeline]].**

### 2026-04-23 — topic-pages retirement + in-page article filtering  ^entry-2026-04-23-topic-pages-retirement-in-page-article

**2026-04-23 topic-pages retirement + in-page article filtering** — In response to Kanwar's UX directive ("clicking a section on the left should filter the studies on the currently displayed page, not take me to an empty new page") and a GSC 404 flag on `/topics/sports-medicine` (root cause: sitemap declared 8 topic URLs but `app/topics/[slug]/page.tsx` routeData keyed 4 of them under different slugs — `sports` vs `sports-medicine`, `hand` vs `hand-wrist`, `pediatrics` vs `pediatric-orthopedics`, `tumor` vs `orthopedic-oncology`). Shipped in one commit: (1) `app/articles/page.tsx` rewritten as a client component with filter state — topic sidebar is buttons not links, live search over title + abstract + authors + topic + type, Article Type (Case Report / Case Series) filter section DELETED per Kanwar ("serves no value here"), URL reflects active topic via `router.replace('/articles?topic=<slug>', { scroll: false })` with a Suspense boundary around `useSearchParams`, initial topic state seeded from the query param so 301 redirects land on a pre-filtered view, article-card topic pill becomes a filter button, clear-all affordance when a filter is active, graceful empty state. (2) `app/topics/page.tsx` + `app/topics/[slug]/page.tsx` DELETED. (3) `app/sitemap.ts` sheds 9 entries (the `/topics` index + 8 subspecialty slugs) — URL count 46 → 37. (4) `next.config.js` gains a `redirects()` block with 6 entries: 4 explicit legacy-route-key → descriptive-slug mappings (`/topics/sports → /articles?topic=sports-medicine`, and the same for `hand` / `pediatrics` / `tumor`) + a generic `/topics/:slug → /articles?topic=:slug` passthrough + `/topics → /articles`. All permanent (301). (5) `lib/constants.ts` SUBSPECIALTIES list left UNCHANGED — its short slugs (`sports`, `hand`, `tumor`, `pediatrics`) still drive the submission wizard, reviewer application form, and Supabase storage across 15 files; the `/articles` filter UI maintains its own descriptive slug list because URLs ≠ DB keys. The comment block in `lib/constants.ts` is updated to document the separation. Side benefits: the 4-URL topic-slug-mismatch bug is resolved (the routes no longer exist so nothing 404s), the "Duplicate without user-selected canonical" risk shrinks, the search input on `/articles` starts working, and the `/topics` tree stops consuming crawl budget. `tsc --noEmit` clean after `.next` cache clear. Route count 66 → 64.
